       NB against TAN in the ERROR DAG (router class mattered there: 44 / -9 / -54 at ply 2, T 0.1), one leaf kind,
       after a ply-2 gain exists to modulate. Last.
    5. Leaf type inside the tree: stage 1 already gives it across the two fields (F-P/F-L each with its own
       trees), with the field effect as the caveat; a mixed 8-line field would cost 13 CPU-h to remove that caveat
       for a question (the probit copy's hierarchical prior, 6.14) that has no ply-2 gain to explain yet. Park.
    So stage 2 is one field (arm 1) plus the offline test in (c), which should run FIRST because it decides whether
    any knob can recover the gap. Design rules that stand: comparator in the field; read at fixed versions (v300,
    v600) rather than at the stop; a field at the floor 600 is ~2.7 h wall at 16 s a cycle, so both stage-1 fields
    land around 19:45.
(c) Theory, one line: a phase partition corrects the SHALLOW-play value function (blunder rates and conversion
    differ by phase under ply-1 play, so P(outcome | features) bends by phase and leaves per phase straighten it);
    under ply-2 play conversion is more uniform, the flat's residual is nearly phase-free, and the leaves have
    little left to learn, slowly (each on a third of the data). Competing hypothesis H2: the gain is there at ply
    2 but the league's training loop reaches it late (data per leaf; 6.32's T 0.1 crownings; still closing at
    v399). The discriminating experiment, no games: train F-P and T-P-Ne OFFLINE on a ply-1 pool (error-dag-probit
    -p1's games) and a ply-2 pool (probit-late-p2's or base-probit-p2's games), equal positions (~30M), same passes,
    and compare tree-minus-flat held-out log-loss (nats per decisive position, SE by game) on each pool, per leaf.
    H1 predicts the ply-2 gain at a third or less of the ply-1 gain at equal data, concentrated in the endgame
    leaves; H2 predicts equal gains, in which case the league's tie is the loop's (data, time, T 0.1 gate) and arm 1
    plus longer runs will recover it. Under H1 stage-2 knobs cannot help and the work moves to what the leaves can
    represent (S7 pairs, 6.26 c-4), which is where the user's "from scratch" should point.
(d) Global league mechanics (cycle 173 at 17:04; 192 cycles by 17:10, ~8 s a cycle):
    1. The Elo window is a noise filter. Per-entry rating SE is ~21 (diff SE ~30 from 190 games an entry a cycle)
       and the eight current versions sit within 892-990, so "Elo minus 30" is one SE and opponents enter or leave
       a model's window by rating noise. The record shows it: a line's training games swing 5 to 1,280 cycle to
       cycle (F-L: 75 of 176 cycles at 245 games or fewer, 32 under 100; the DAGs 8-17 cycles under 100). The
       training sets are effectively random subsets of random size, the "fewest games marks the leader" readout is
       noise at this spread, and the fixed game order makes a 5-game cycle a near-no-op version. Harmless to data
       quality (random references are excluded, which is good), harmful to the pace: the stronger a model's rating
       noise says it is, the less it trains, a negative feedback that compresses the field toward a tie regardless
       of architecture. At ~100k positions a cycle each model has ~15-20M positions now; the ply-1 trees' edge
       formed between 46M and 64M (G2), so no verdict for ~300 more cycles, and the throttle will bite exactly then.
       A margin of 2 SE (60) or a window by the champion's Elo (steadier) would keep the rule and lose the noise;
       the user's call, not a live change.
    2. Crownings are mostly noise after cycle ~40. At 2 SE with 8 tests a cycle the chance rate is 0.18 a cycle;
       observed 41 crownings in cycles 52-160 (0.38 a cycle) with the field flat since ~cycle 40, and the crowned
       champions are winner's-curse versions (every line's current sits within +-40 of its champion at 173). So
       patience by positions never accrues past ~25% and retirement is off in practice; fine for now, but the
       champion entries carry no information and the "champion" Elo is the current's plus noise. Pooling the test
       over cycles since the last crowning (6.32 amendment's form) would fix both at no cost.
    3. Twins: z 30 / z 20 and z 160 / z 120 parted at cycle 2 by the window (5 against 20 games), not by a router
       split (none logged; the DAG training logs carry no split lines at all, so leaf and router counts need a
       treeview read). They are now two seeds of one recipe with different data paths; any z contrast in this
       league is unpaired and confounded with the data order, and if z 20 / z 120 never split the four DAG lines
       are four error trees. Read them as replicates of the error family, not as a threshold test.
    4. Smaller: the cycle report's "patience positions this cycle" is the previous version's training (it lags the
       "trained on" column by a cycle: 245 games -> 32,063); and the cluster trees are the only lines with any
       structural trace in the logs (T-P-Nc one split held, gain 0.004-0.006 nats; T-L-Nc one split merged).

6.35 (17:30, 11-11). The offline discriminator for (c) of 6.34, fixed design and prediction, filed before anything
runs. The main session's draft stands with these changes.
Pools (positions, not games, are the unit; T 0.1; round robin of F-P#1/#2 and T-P-Ne#1/#2 as in the fields):
  P1  = G2 finals (error-routing-p1 v314) at ply 1;  P2 = probit-late-p2 finals (v399) at ply 2;
  P2x = G2 finals at ply 2 (the draft's control);    P1x = late-p2 finals at ply 1 (4 CPU-min; completes the 2x2
  players x depth, so "play depth" and "player quality" each get a paired contrast).
  Size 24M positions each (not 6M): with the 1M threshold the league's trees had 7 leaves at ~10M and 9 at ~20M
  (G2 v49/v99), so 6M would compare 3-5-leaf trees and lose the second level, where the ply-1 edge formed. Cost:
  ply 1 ~5 CPU-min a pool, ply 2 ~55; four pools ~2 CPU-h, training minutes (hybrid6: 145k positions in 0.8 s).
  Held-out 10% BY GAME (last 10% of games in the shuffled order; ~2.4M positions, ~10-20k games): the gain SE with
  clustering by game is ~0.0003-0.0005, so a contrast of two pools resolves ~0.001 nats.
Training: on each pool, from scratch, F-P and T-P-Ne with the fields' exact flags (--leaf probit --router cnbp
  --route error --error log --split-threshold 1000000 --split-limit 4 --split-noise 0.03 --use-probs, non-merging),
  ONE pass in one seeded game order, the same order for both models (the league is one pass over fresh games; a
  second pass measures memorisation, not the field). Replicate by a second ORDER seed on P1 and P2 only if the
  main contrast lands within 2 SE of its decision line. Record the leaf count and each split's version.
Scores, per pool: held-out full log-loss per position (nats; all positions, the training objective) for both
  models, g = flat minus tree; winner log-loss as the secondary (6.29: its gains never carried into play, so a
  winner-only gain is not evidence); per-leaf g (route held-out positions through the tree's routers, flat loss
  minus tree loss within each leaf, leaves labelled by mean material / piece count so "endgame" is readable).
  Transfer, four extra scorings, no training: the P1 pair scored on P2's held-out and on P2x's; the P2 pair on
  P1's and P1x's. This is the sharpest cut: it asks whether the correction a ply-1 tree learned is a correction for
  ply-2 OUTCOMES at all (H1 says no), independent of whether a ply-2 learner can find it (H2's claim).
Decision lines, with g1 = g on P1 (expected 0.006-0.012 nats for 9 leaves: the league's one-split revision gains
  are 0.004-0.006), g2 = g on P2:
  H1 (the field's play): g2 / g1 at or under 1/3 AND the P1 tree's gain scored on P2 at or under 1/3 of g1; P2x
     near P2 (ratio to g1 under 1/2) and P1x near P1: depth, not the players, sets the headroom.
  H2 (the loop is slow): g2 / g1 at or over 2/3 (within SE) AND the P1 tree keeps 2/3 of g1 on P2; then the
     league's tie is data, time and the T 0.1 gate, and arm 1 of 6.34 (b) plus longer runs recover it.
  Between 1/3 and 2/3: both, in proportion; then expect a ply-2 edge of roughly 118 x g2 / g1 Elo at best (the
     P1 rate, 118 Elo for g1), i.e. 40-60 if the ratio is 1/2, and arm 1 is still worth one field.
  Side readouts that decide the follow-up either way: the P2 tree's leaf count (5-7 under H1, since trials fail
  when there is less to gain; 9 under H2) and the per-leaf share (H1: over 60% of g2 in the lowest-material
  leaves; H2: spread as at P1).
Prediction, dated: g1 0.006-0.012; g2 0.001-0.004 with g2 / g1 0.15-0.4; P2x within 0.002 of P2 and P1x within
  0.002 of P1; P1 tree on P2 under 0.3 g1; P2 tree on P1 0.3-0.6 g1 (a ply-2 tree finds part of the ply-1
  structure); P2 tree at 5-7 leaves, P1 tree at 7-9; winner log-loss gains smaller than full-loss gains on P1 and
  about equal on P2. If g1 itself comes in under 0.004 the test is underpowered on this held-out size and the
  pools should double before any reading; if g2 comes in ABOVE g1 the theory is wrong outright and the ply-2 tie
  is entirely the loop.

6.36 (18:20, 11-11). Growth: why splits stop, benefit-based rules, and the offline comparison. Source checked:
rs/src/delegation.rs (leaf rule), models/from-scratch/bddtrig-src/src/bdd.rs (router rule with --router-split-z).
Interim on 6.35 first: P1-on-P1 g1 = 0.088 nats (flat 0.7565, tree 0.6684, SE 0.0012; 68% of it in the 43%-of-
positions low-material leaf), P1x-on-P1x 0.056. The magnitude band of 6.35 (0.006-0.012) was wrong by 7x: the
one-pass offline flat is far less calibrated than a league flat (its held-out loss is above ln 2), so the tree's
calibration-by-phase is most of g here; the ratios of 6.35 still decide, and g1 is powered. Noted, not revised.
(1) Mechanism, corrected. Leaf splits use SplitRule::Worse with split_z 0 (the recipes set no --split-z): at every
    1M-example window close a leaf splits iff the window's mean error exceeds the previous window's, by ANY amount,
    until split_limit. Early, while the leaf learns, errors fall and it cannot fire; once the leaf has converged the
    comparison is a coin flip (p ~0.5 a check under stationarity), so the rule is not a drift detector but noise plus
    a cap: the cap is the only brake, and splits land at random times (the offline P1 tree, on a stationary shuffled
    pool with no field drift, still spent its four by v80: v20, v36, v66, v80). Expected uncapped rate: the checks per
    stream position sum to 1 per 1M whatever the leaf count, so ~0.5 splits per 1M stream positions, exponential-
    looking only because every split makes a new converged leaf. Router splits (bddtrig bdd.rs 222-230) need a RISE
    of more than router_split_z SE between consecutive windows AND the router's error at or above the routers' mean
    AND budget. At z 0 that is the same coin flip gated by above-mean (p ~0.25 a check), spent early; at z 20-30
    (probit) or 120-160 (LR) a rise of that many SE needs a systematic shift of the error-routing target, which only
    the field's drift supplies (the opponents and games change fast early), so those z are drift detectors that fire
    early or never, and in a stationary pool never. The above-mean condition favours the root (the widest, worst
    router), which is why every router split so far was the root's or a copy's. Neither rule measures benefit, and
    neither can fire late on a stationary router however much a split would help. The coordinator's read holds for
    routers; for leaves the correction is that z 0 fires on noise, not only on change, and the cap, not the quiet
    field, is what ends growth (the 1M threshold spends limit 4 by ~15-25M positions, v100-v150 in a league field).
(2) A benefit test for router splits that can fire at any time: PATH-SHADOW ROUTERS. A router R with path context
    is reached by m arrival paths (its contexts). Keep, beside the shared router, one shadow router per arrival path
    (the copies a split would create), each trained only on its path's examples, with the same forgetting. Error
    routing already computes every child's loss on each example (the routing target), so the per-example benefit of
    the path-specific choice is free: gain(x) = loss[child chosen by R](x) - loss[child chosen by shadow_p](x). Keep
    the window mean of gain over R's examples and its SE from 20 blocks (route_switch's statistic, delegation.rs
    266-284; block SE because a game's positions are correlated). At each window close split iff mean gain > z SE
    (z 2) AND mean gain > the MDL cost of the new routers, (m-1)*d_R*ln(n)/(2n) nats per example (d_R the router's
    parameter count, n the examples through R; at n 1M and d_R ~800 per copy that is ~0.006, at 10M ~0.0007: the bar
    falls with data, so a split that pays becomes allowed as evidence accumulates). On a split the shadows BECOME the
    routers (already trained; no forgetting restart), the trial counter resets, and a failed window costs nothing but
    the shadows' upkeep. Exact and deterministic (same statistic every run), computable in the loop, cost m router
    updates per example at R, i.e. linear in routers x paths, which at branch factor 3 is at most 3x the routing
    cost at that node. The root has one path and never router-splits under this test, which is right: every root
    copy so far stacked the phase split and gained nothing (6.29, 6.32 note). Prediction for the test itself: on
    ply-1 data it fires rarely (out-of-sample re-routing headroom measured ~0, 6.28 correction), and that is the
    correct answer, not a defect; it fires when leaves differ by path, which is what growth past 9 leaves needs.
(3) Leaf growth without a count cap: SPLIT ON TRIAL WITH AN MDL BAR. The code already has split_trial (delegation.rs
    249-259): the split node trains beside a shadow copy of the unsplit leaf for the next split_threshold examples,
    and the split is kept iff the branch beats the shadow; undone splits do not count. Make it the rule: (i) every
    leaf is tried every split_threshold of its own examples once converged (same window close as now; the rise test
    goes, --split-limit inf); (ii) keep iff mean gain over the shadow in full log-loss (trial_error = the training
    objective, not Winner) exceeds 2 block-SE (the trial's SE today is per example, which the comment itself says
    understates the noise; use the 20-block SE as revise does) AND exceeds (B-1)*d_L*ln(n)/(2n), the description
    length of the new leaves (B-1 leaves of d_L parameters, n examples through the node): at 1M examples ~0.010
    nats, at 10M ~0.0012, at 100M ~0.00014; (iii) after a failed trial the leaf's next trial waits twice as long
    (1M, 2M, 4M, ...), so a leaf with nothing to gain costs O(log data) trials and the shadows in flight stay at most
    one per leaf: cost linear in leaves, under 2x a leaf's training. Bar falls with data (the user's "evidence test
    whose bar scales with data"), noise is held at 2 SE, and the structure can grow at any time a region has
    earned it. Elo given error-growth-p1: limit 8 was 25 below limit 4 at ply 1 and level at ply 2, with the third
    level mostly copies (G nil); under this rule those third-level splits fail their trials (a copy pair gains ~0
    < bar) and are undone, so the rule should reproduce limit 4's Elo within 10 at ply 1 with 9-11 leaves, not
    limit 8's 17, and at ply 2 it should hold 3-7 leaves (less to gain per split), removing the fragmentation that
    the capped rule imposes there. The gain it adds over limit 4 at ply 1 by 300 cycles: 0-15; its value is that
    growth continues when (and only when) the leaves have more to learn, e.g. with S7 features.
(4) Offline comparison on the discriminator's pools (offline-ply: P1, P1x, P2, P2x, 21.6M train each, held-out by
    game), tree arm T-P-Ne and DAG arm D-P-Ne-ctx, one pass, same order; filed before any run:
    A. current tree rule, limit 4 (P1, P1x done: 4 splits at v20/36/66/80 and v20/35/55/56).
    B. current rule, --split-limit 1000 (flag only, run now): the uncapped coin flip. Prediction: 7-12 splits per
       pool (15-25 leaves), continuing to the last round at ~0.5 per 1M; held-out loss within -0.003..+0.010 of A
       (worse, if at all, by fragmentation), with sibling cross-fit showing copies at the third level and below.
    C. the MDL trial rule of (3), limit inf (code): 4-6 splits on P1/P1x (9-13 leaves), first at v10-v20 and the
       last before v90, 2-4 on P2/P2x (5-9 leaves); held-out within 0.003 of A on P1/P1x and 0.000-0.005 better
       than A on P2/P2x (fewer, earned leaves); undone trials logged: 2-6 per pool, all at the third level.
    D. DAG, current router rule at z 20 (probit) and at z 0: in a stationary pool z 20 makes NO router split on any
       pool (no drift, no 20-SE rise); z 0 spends its 4 by ~v40-v60 on the root and its copies; loss within 0.002
       of A either way (copies route like the original).
    E. DAG, path-shadow rule of (2) at z 2 with the MDL bar (code): 0-1 router splits on P1/P1x, 0 on P2/P2x; loss
       within 0.001 of A; the logged shadow gains stay under 0.001 at every router after v30. A router split that
       fires AND lowers held-out loss by 0.002 or more would be the first measured routing benefit here.
    Measures: split and router-split counts with versions, held-out g against the pool's flat and against A, per-
    leaf g, sibling cross-fit D_jk at the end, undone-trial log. Cost: B and D are flags (minutes each on hybrid6 /
    hybrid15); C and E need the fork. If B reproduces its prediction the mechanism in (1) is demonstrated without
    any new code, and that is the result to show the user first.

6.37 (18:40, 11-11). Growth rules to the user's requirement (18:05: wait for stagnation, split at a constant rate
per unit of data, the benefit test chooses). One rule set for leaves and routers.
(i) Stagnation (eligibility). Every node keeps its windowed loss over its OWN examples, window W = split_threshold
    (1M), mean and SE from 20 blocks of W/20 (the block SE of revise/route_switch; per-example SEs understate the
    game correlation). For a leaf the loss is its prediction's full log-loss; for a router its routing error (the
    chosen child's loss, already recorded as router_error). At a window close the node is STAGNANT iff the last K =
    2 consecutive improvements were each insignificant: L[t-1] - L[t] <= z_s * sqrt(se[t-1]^2 + se[t]^2) with z_s =
    2, for t and t-1. So a node needs 3 own windows (3M own examples) before it can be eligible: the root at 3M
    stream positions, a level-1 leaf at 9M after its creation, a level-2 leaf at 27M; a rising loss (drift) counts
    as not improving, which keeps today's cases eligible but no longer sufficient. Exact (sums in fixed order),
    O(1) per example per node.
(ii) Allowance (rate). The model keeps an integer credit counter: one credit per D training positions through the
    ROOT (stream positions, so the rate is per unit of the model's data whatever its size), capped at C = 2 so
    credits cannot pile up while nothing is eligible and then burst. A credit is spent when a split is MADE (leaf:
    when its trial starts; refunded if the trial fails, so failed trials do not consume the rate); at most C trials
    in flight. Setting D: at a constant rate the leaf count is L ~ 1 + (B-1) N / D, so the mean data per leaf tends
    to D / (B-1) = D/2 at branch factor 3: D fixes the steady-state examples per leaf, not just the pace. The ply-1
    gains formed at 5-7M examples per leaf (G2's 9 leaves at 46-64M), so D = 10M (mean 5M per leaf; one split per
    50 ply-1 cycles at 200k, 70 ply-2 cycles at 146k, 100 global-league cycles at 100k) is the lean default and D =
    5M the fast one; today's capped regime was D ~5M for 20M positions and then zero. Timeline at D 10M, 1M windows,
    K 2 in a ply-1 field: 3 leaves at ~6M (v30: the root is stagnant at 3-5M and the first credit comes at 10M, so
    the first split is at 10M, v50), 5 at 20M, 7 at 30M (v150), 9 at 40M (v200), level-2 leaves eligible 27M
    after creation, so 11-13 leaves by v500 if they pass the benefit test (at ply 1 most will not: copies).
(iii) Choice and keep (benefit). When a credit exists and at least one node is stagnant: ROUTERS first if any
    multi-path router's path-shadow gain (6.36 (2)) already clears 2 block-SE and the MDL cost (m-1) d_R ln(n) /
    (2n): its split is made at once and the credit spent (its evidence is in hand; no trial). Otherwise the LEAF
    with the largest excess of window loss over the model's mean loss over the same window (StallHigh's ordering:
    split where the model is worst; ties by most examples, then lowest index) starts a split ON TRIAL: the split
    node trains beside a shadow copy of the unsplit leaf for one window of its own examples; keep iff mean gain
    over the shadow in full log-loss > 2 block-SE AND > (B-1) d_L ln(n) / (2n) (0.010 nats at n 1M, 0.0012 at
    10M, 0.00014 at 100M: the bar falls with data); else undo, refund the credit, and the leaf's next eligibility
    waits twice its last wait (1, 2, 4 windows). Exact and deterministic throughout (every statistic is a sum over a
    fixed-order stream; every choice a total order with index tie-break; credits are integers). Cost: windows O(1)
    per example per node; at most C shadow leaves in flight (<= 2 extra leaf updates per example); router shadows m
    per multi-path router (<= 3x that node's routing cost). Linear in size.
Size cap in play: hybrid15's fixed-point path is exact up to 64 nodes and falls back to float (exact, hybrid14's
    speed) above. With branch factor 3 every split (leaf or router) adds 3 nodes: nodes = 1 + 3 s, so 64 nodes is
    21 splits (43 leaves), reached at D 10M after ~210M positions (ply-1 cycle ~1000, longer at ply 2) and at D 5M
    after ~105M; no current run gets there, and the league's priced CPU shows the fallback if one does. The engine
    should log the node count at every split and warn at 55.
Offline test (the discriminator's pools, 21.6M train each, one pass, hybrid6-class code with the new rule):
    runs: P1 and P2 at D 2.5M and D 5M (four runs), K 2, z_s 2, trial z 2 + MDL; plus B of 6.36 (uncapped coin
    flip) as the contrast and A (limit 4) as the reference. Predictions, filed before any run:
    - Timing: root stagnant at 3-5M (v15-v25) on every pool; first split at the first credit after that: 5M (v25)
      at D 5M, 5M at D 2.5M too (credits at 2.5M wait); level-1 leaves stagnant ~9M after creation (~14M, v70);
      at D 5M credits at 10M (held), 15M (spent: trial to ~18M, kept), 20M (spent, trial unfinished at v108): 2
      kept splits (5 leaves) by v108; at D 2.5M: 3-4 kept (7-9 leaves) by v108, bounded by stagnation (two trials
      in flight from ~14M, the next pair from ~18M), none at level 2. Kept splits against positions lie on or under
      the 1/D line in every run; the B run shows 7-12 by v108 on the same pools.
    - Benefit: on P1/P1x every level-1 trial passes (gains 0.01-0.1 nats against a bar of ~0.004 at n 3M); on
      P2/P2x 1-2 of 3 pass (smaller phase structure), 0-1 undone. Held-out loss: within 0.003 of A on P1/P1x
      (same partition, made later), within 0.003 of A on P2/P2x. The rule's value does not show in 21.6M positions
      of loss; it shows in the timing list and in what it refuses.
    - In the league (prediction for a ply-1 field, when built): same Elo as limit 4 within 10 at v300 with 9 leaves
      reached ~v200 instead of v100-150 (a 0-10 deficit at v150 that closes), and growth past 9 only where the
      trial passes; a D 10M line at v500 holds 9-13 leaves. If the user wants faster visible growth, D 5M; not
      below, since the mean data per leaf then drops under 2.5M, below where the ply-1 gains formed.

6.37 addendum (18:50), on the coordinator's two questions and the builder's paragraph.
(i) The trial's gain test does NOT imply stagnation, and stagnation must gate the trial. A trial compares the
    split (children on shares of the data) with a shadow of the unsplit leaf on the same future examples; while the
    leaf is still learning fast, the data-splitting cost (each child learns on a third) dominates and the trial
    fails for a reason that has nothing to do with the partition's worth, wasting the credit's window and putting a
    good leaf into back-off. Stagnation is the cost filter that makes the trial's verdict about the partition.
(ii) D against the MDL bar: D sets the mean data per leaf (D/2 at branch factor 3); the bar sets the smallest gain
    a split may keep at that data, (B-1) d ln(n) / (2n): 0.010 at 1M, 0.004 at 3M, 0.0025 at 5M, 0.0012 at 10M.
    Gains under ~0.004 nats have never carried into play (6.29), so D should put the typical trial at n where the
    bar is at or under that: n >= 3M, D >= 6M, and D 5M (trials at 3-5M own examples, the stagnation minimum)
    is the fastest setting that is not wasted on failing trials; D 10M the lean one. Back-off and the constant rate
    do not conflict: 1/D is a ceiling (credits accrue at it and cap at 2), failures refund their credit, and a node
    in back-off simply is not eligible; if nothing is eligible the credits wait and the realised rate falls below
    the ceiling, which is the benefit test refusing growth, as intended. The rate is constant when there is
    something worth splitting and zero when there is not.
Builder's paragraph. Each node keeps a window of W = split_threshold own examples with mean loss and 20-block SE;
    a node is stagnant when its last two window-to-window improvements are each under 2 block-SE (so after 3 own
    windows at least). The model accrues one credit per D root examples (default 5M; a flag), capped at 2. At a
    window close with a credit and a stagnant node: if a multi-path router's path-shadow gain (6.36) exceeds 2
    block-SE and (m-1) d_R ln(n)/(2n), split it now and spend the credit; else take the stagnant leaf whose window
    loss most exceeds the model's (ties: more examples, lower index) and start a split on trial, spending the
    credit: for one further window of its examples the split node trains beside a shadow of the unsplit leaf;
    keep iff the branch's mean gain over the shadow in full log-loss exceeds 2 block-SE and (B-1) d_L ln(n)/(2n),
    else undo, refund, and double that leaf's wait before its next eligibility. At most 2 trials in flight. No
    split limit; log the node count at every split and warn at 55 (hybrid15 leaves fixed point above 64 nodes).
Predicted leaf curves (branch factor 3, W 1M, K 2). Offline 21.6M positions: D 5M: 3 leaves at 5M (root stagnant
    by 3-5M), two level-1 trials from ~15M, 5-7 leaves kept by 21.6M with one trial open; D 2.5M: 7-9 by 21.6M,
    bounded by the 9M-after-creation stagnation of level-1 leaves, none at level 2. League, ply 1 at 200k a
    cycle, D 5M: 3 leaves at v25, 7 at ~v90, 9 at ~v115 (about today's pace for the first four, then no stop),
    level-2 leaves eligible from ~v250 with trials mostly failing at ply 1 (copies): 9-15 leaves at v600, 2-6
    undone trials. Ply 2 at 146k, D 5M: 3 at v35, 9 at ~v160, 9-13 at v600. D 10M halves every slope.

6.38 (18:55, 11-11). Arm F (T-P-Ne flags plus --split-rule stall-high --split-z 2 --split-limit 1000, one pass on
P1/P1x/P2/P2x), filed before it runs. The rule: split at a window close iff the leaf's error did not fall by more
than 2 SE since the previous window AND its window error is at least the whole model's over that window; the SE is
per example (not block), so on game-correlated positions z 2 is effectively z ~0.7-1. In a stationary pool a
converged leaf stalls at almost every check, so the binding condition is "above the model's mean error": F splits
the high-loss region (many-piece middlegame leaves, whose outcomes are least decided) and never the low-loss,
high-gain endgame leaf (P1's leaf 4, 43% of positions, 68% of g1). Prediction: root at v15-v25 (stalls at 3-5M);
level-1 children stall from ~2 own windows (~6M stream after the split), the 1-2 above-mean ones split at
~v50-v65; their above-mean children at ~v80-v105: 5-8 splits per pool (11-17 leaves) by v108, lopsided (depth 3-4
in the middlegame branch, depth 1 in the endgame), still splitting at the end; about the same on P2/P2x (one fewer
if anything). Loss: within 0.003 of A on every pool; against B, -0.002 to +0.001 (F fragments less where the loss
is low, so a hair better than B, both near A: offline, copies cost nothing). As a stand-in for 6.37: F is the
stagnation GATE in a weak form (one window, per-example SE) with 6.37's StallHigh ordering hardened into a gate
(above-mean regions only), and nothing of the allowance or the trial: its rate is the check rate of above-mean
leaves (~0.5 per 1M stream, like B's), not a rate per data, and it keeps every split it makes. The 11-05 steady
pace (rounds 9, 82, 108, ...) came from the field's drift plus that gate, not from a rate rule. A fair test of
eligibility, not of the rule.

6.38 note (19:20, 11-11), two facts for the stage-1 read, no reply sent.
(1) Router splits at ply 2 went the REVERSE of 6.34 (a): both probit z 20 DAGs in base-probit-p2 had spent all 4
    router splits by v310, and neither LR z 120 DAG in base-lr-p2 had any. The LR rises shrank below 120 SE at ply
    2 as the note expected for both kinds; the probit rises did not shrink below 20. Likely cause: the ply-2 probit
    field is far from its plateau for hundreds of cycles (6.33: F-P gained 122 at ply 2 between v241 and v399), so
    the routing target drifts for longer and harder than in a ply-1 field, and z 20 sat just under the ply-1 cliff
    (20-25) to begin with. Consequences for the read: the probit DAG lines are BUSY DAGs (expect root copies, as
    always) and carry the ply-1 busy-DAG risk (z 0: -41 at a ply-2 read), so "DAG minus cluster tree" there is a
    router-split cost test, not a tie test; the LR DAG lines are exactly the LR error trees of their seed (twins), so
    base-lr-p2 reads "LR error tree against LR cluster tree against F-L" at ply 2, which is a cleaner question than
    the one planned. The prediction's miss is recorded; the Elo bands of 6.34 stand.
(2) The global league retired T-P-Nc-merge-fresh.p1 at cycle 532: 20.0M positions without a crowning since cycle
    355 (champion G.22.354 at 967; current 934 at retirement, inside the field's 892-990 and within noise of its
    champion). This is 6.34 (d)(2) in action, not a strength verdict: the champion is a winner's-curse version
    (crowned on a noise high), so the current must beat it by 2 SE (~60) from ~33 behind, a ~3-SE event per cycle;
    177 cycles without one is then likely, and the line with the LUCKIEST champion is the one retired. P(no chance
    crowning in 177 cycles) ~1.6% per line, ~12% that one of eight goes that long. Expect more of the same unless
    the crowning is pooled over cycles (6.32 amendment) or the test runs against the champion's pooled rating.
    The cluster tree itself had kept splitting and merging (1-4 splits) to v524, the only line with live structure.

6.38 addendum (19:35). F4 (stall-high z 2, limit 4), before it runs: root at v15-v25, then the above-mean level-1
children at ~v50-v65 and a fourth at ~v65-v85, so all 4 spent by ~v70-v90 (A: v80 on P1, v56 on P1x), lopsided
(3 of 4 in the high-loss middlegame branch, the endgame leaf unsplit); held-out loss within 0.003 of A on every
pool, more likely 0.000-0.002 worse than better. F4 minus A is the rule's placement effect alone; a difference
beyond 0.003 either way would be the first evidence that WHERE the capped splits go matters offline.
League version of the stall-rule test. Arms: F-P x2 (comparator), T-P-Ne current rule (worse / z 0, limit 4) x2,
T-P-Ne stall-high z 2 limit 4 x2: the one factor is the leaf split rule, cap held at 4 so timing and placement are
the only differences (the uncapped stall rule is the next field, if F4 is not worse). Tree form, not DAG: the stall
rule is a BDT leaf rule and the DAG's routers have their own. One field, shared train seed as usual; a second seed
only if the contrast exceeds 20. Ply: run it at PLY 1 first (1 CPU-h, floor 300, reads at plies 1-2): there the
tree gain exists (~100) and the rule's timing shows in Elo; at ply 2 every tree rule so far ties flat, so a
split-rule contrast there (10 CPU-h) would measure a tie among ties unless a rule creates a gain, which no offline
arm predicts. Give the ply-2 slot to stage-2 arm 1 (limit 1 against 4). If the user wants the stall rule at ply 2
regardless: the same six lines, floor 600, reads at v300 / v600 / final, prediction all within 20 of F-P at ply 2
and stall-high within 15 of the current rule at every ply; at ply 1 the prediction is stall-high minus current
-15 to 10 at v300 (later splits lose a little early data to the second level, nothing by v300).

6.37 note (19:45), consecutive router splits and fresh windows. Checked in bddtrig bdd.rs split_delegator: the
split node gets a fresh router and forget_window() (error_prev -1, so its next window cannot fire), and the copies
are new_node's with empty windows; windows are NOT inherited today. The cascade (z 20: v54, v109, v125, v135; z 0:
4 by v49-v79) is the coordinator's mechanism in the SPLIT NODE: the root keeps a full-stream window (1M, ~5
cycles), is eligible again after one blank window, and its routing target among copies that forget and diverge is
new and moving, so its error rises and it fires again; the copies (a third of the stream each) rarely get there
first. Under 6.37 the user's intuition holds by construction for copies (3 own windows = 9M stream at level 1) but
not for the split node itself (full stream, 3M); add to the spec: a node that has just split (leaf or router) is
ineligible until every node the split made has closed three own windows. With the credit spacing, two consecutive
splits at one node are then impossible. Spec of starts: a split-copy router and a new leaf copy the parent's
PARAMETERS (forgotten by forget_factor, leaves with split noise) and start with EMPTY window statistics (counts,
sums, blocks, previous-window marks: all zero, no previous), as the code already does; shadow routers start as
parameter copies of the shared router (so their gain is 0 until they specialise) with empty gain windows, keep
training across windows (no parameter reset after a failed window), and only the gain statistic restarts.

6.39 (19:55, 11-11). The user's "more stalled than its children" (19:30). Agreed on both readings: taken literally
it admits the cascade (right after a split the children learn fast, the parent looks the more stalled, and its
routing target is moving because of them); the intent is SUBTREE STALL: a node is eligible only when it and every
descendant are stagnant (each node's last two own-window improvements under 2 block-SE), which for leaves is plain
stagnation and for routers means the routing target is stable when the shadow gain is judged. It implies and
replaces the 6.37-note patch (a just-split node waits for its split's nodes). Tolerance, improved: weight by
examples, eligible iff stagnant nodes held at least 90% of the subtree's examples in the last window; the small
leaf that never settles is also the one that barely moves the target, so the weighting is principled, not a fudge.
Effects. Leaf curve of 6.37: unchanged (leaves have no subtree). Routers: bottom-up by construction; a level-1
router over three leaves is eligible 3 own windows after its last leaf split (9M own = 27M stream at a third), the
root only when every leaf has settled: at D 5M, ply 1 (200k a cycle), the last level-1 split at ~v115 puts the
root's first eligibility at ~v250, and every later leaf split under it blocks it again for ~27M stream (~135
cycles), so root router splits become rare and low routers split first, which is what the evidence wants (every
root copy so far gained nothing). Credits are not consumed by waiting, so the leaf rate is unaffected.
Correction to 6.36 (2) that this exposes: a path-shadow test needs several arrival paths, and in a DAG grown from a
tree no router has them until some router above has split, so the root (one path) would never split and nothing
could start. Fix: the shadow PARTITION is the arrival path where there are several, and otherwise a shadow cluster
router (NB hard-EM, label-free, as cluster routing trains) over the node's examples, with one shadow routing copy
per cluster; gain(x) = loss[R's child] - loss[copy_c(x)'s child], same 2 block-SE and MDL bar; cost 4 router updates
per example at R. On a split the shadow cluster router becomes R's new top router and the copies its delegators,
already trained. This is the split the code makes, tested in shadow before it is made.

6.39 note (20:20). The consolidated builder spec for the growth rule (6.36-6.39: stagnation, subtree stall with
the 90% example-weighted share, fresh starts, credits at one per D positions capped at 2, trial with block SE and
the MDL bar, back-off, path or cluster shadows for routers, unsharing by path, the 55-node warning, model-file
and state contents, identity requirement, unit tests, offline arms G/H and the ply-1 league test) is
models/from-scratch/theory-2/growth-spec.md. Phase 1 is leaves (BDT), phase 2 routers (BDD).

6.40 (20:45, 11-11). Router trigger: stall AND headroom (the user, 19:50). Source checked: fastbin/errdag4-src
bdd.rs 338-375, delegation.rs 1489-1512 and 2188-2210, bdt.rs 160-180.
(1) The user's reading is right. Under subtree stall a router waits until stagnant nodes hold 90% of its examples,
    and every kept leaf split below it adds nodes that need three own windows (a level-1 leaf 9M stream, a level-2
    leaf 27M), while each trial in flight (one own window of the split node, 3M stream at level 1) un-stalls it
    too. At 100k positions a cycle with D 5M (a credit every 50 cycles), the first level-1 router is eligible
    ~27M after the last split under it (~cycle 300-350) and the root ~cycle 500-600, and in a field whose leaf
    trials keep passing the root is never eligible: routers are last in line by construction, and with a bar that
    falls like ln(n)/n leaves can keep earning splits. The spec's phase 2 eligibility is replaced as follows.
(2) Trigger. Two statistics per router v over its last window, both with 20-block SE:
    - headroom H_v = mean over scored examples of e[routed child](x) - min_k e_k(x): the oracle re-routing gain
      among the CURRENT children. Optimistic (a minimum over B noisy children), so it is a NECESSARY condition
      only: if even the oracle cannot beat the bar, no router split can.
    - realised gain G_v = the prequential gain of the shadow partition of growth-spec 2.2-2.3 (path where the
      node has several arrival paths, else a label-free cluster shadow), scored on each example BEFORE the
      shadows train on it: out of sample by construction, no held-out half needed and no null to subtract.
    Eligible iff stagnant(v) on its own routing error (0.2 of the spec; subtree stall dropped) AND every direct
    child has closed at least one window (no judging over children in their first window; this is the cascade
    guard) AND H_v > 2 se_H AND H_v > bar_mdl(k, n_v). Split iff eligible AND G_v > 2 se_G AND G_v > bar_mdl(k,
    n_v), k the router parameters the split adds (spec 0.7). The MDL bar: at n 10M and k ~2,500 (B copies of a
    Cnbp over 70 features x 13 values), 2,500 x 16.1 / 2e7 = 0.002 nats; at 3M 0.006.
    Credits: one stream. A router whose G_v passes takes the credit before any leaf trial (evidence in hand beats
    a prospective trial); among routers, the largest (G_v - bar) / k, the user's gain per parameter; a leaf's gain
    is unknown before its trial, so leaves do not compete on that scale and take the credit only when no router
    passes. No trial after a router split: G_v already IS the split judged out of sample; keep the post-split check
    with back-off (spec 2.5). The shadows run only while H_v has cleared its bars in the last window (so their
    cost is paid only where a split could pay), and they persist across windows once started.
(3) Cost from the code. Error routers (Ne): every scored example already computes every child's SUBTREE error
    (prediction_errors over all children, bdd.rs 362-366; with shared children routing_errors computes the shared
    set once), so H_v is free and G_v costs m shadow router evaluations and updates at v (NB over 70 features).
    Along ctx paths predict_training(child) scores the child under the current descent's path cells, so the
    errors are the losses the DAG would realise on that path: the right quantity. Cluster routers (Nc): only the
    routed child's error is computed (bdd.rs 368-371, bdt.rs 175-178), so H_v would cost B - 1 extra subtree
    descents per scored example at each cluster router, about doubling the forward cost of training at that node;
    phase 2 is for Ne (the eight's DAGs), where the cost is the shadows only.
(4) Prediction. The out-of-sample re-routing gain measured at existing ply-1 leaves was 0.0011 and -0.0000 per
    decisive position at the cells, largest per router 0.0019 +-0.0012 (6.28 correction): under both bars (2 SE
    ~0.0024, MDL 0.002 at 10M). So G_v would not pass on today's ply-1 models, and H_v (the oracle) I expect at
    5-20x G_v, 0.005-0.03, which would pass its gate and start shadows that then fail: the rule would run its
    shadows and not fire. Is that right? For re-routing among the existing leaves, yes. The user's objection, that
    a static measure understates a split whose children go on to specialise, is real in principle, and the
    evidence we have on it is the root copies: given the chance to specialise (z 0 DAGs, four router splits by
    v50-v80, copies that forget and retrain), they gained nothing in play (0 at ply 1, -41 at ply 2) and sat within
    +-0.0015 of a same-leaves tree offline in five studies. So for root copies the dynamic gain was nil too. For
    the untested case, unsharing a LOWER router by path, the offline test is cheap: arm H' forces one unsharing at
    a level-1 router of the P1 DAG at v60 regardless of the test and trains 10M more positions beside the unforced
    run; a held-out gain of 0.002 or more would say the static gate is too strict and G_v should be measured over
    a longer shadow life (shadows that specialise for several windows before judgement, which the design already
    allows: they persist). Prediction for H': within 0.001 of the unforced run.
(5) Global league games. SE 33 on current-minus-champion at 150 games an entry; more games buy SE by the square
    root at linear cost, so pooling is the lever: K = 10 NON-OVERLAPPING cycles, test every tenth cycle on the
    pooled paired diffs (1,500 games an entry, SE ~10, learning bias under 10 Elo late in a run) at 2 SE; a sliding
    window would need a 2.5 SE bar for the repeated correlated tests. Training floor from a 5-cycle EMA of the
    current's rating. Retirement (F-P at 663 with its current 23 ahead, z 0.73) is then judged on the same pooled
    test, which removes the winner's-curse champions that caused both retirements so far.

1. ROUTER-SPLIT EXPERIMENT (router-p1). One factor, the router-split trigger, at four levels in one ply-1 field.
   Recipe: D-P-Ne-ctx (probit leaves, NB error routers, path context): the family with the largest and best

     H  stall + headroom only: split when the router's routing error is stagnant, its direct children have closed
        a window, and the oracle headroom H_v clears 2 block-SE and the MDL bar (no shadow): the optimistic gate
        as a trigger, the contrast that tells whether G_v's refusals are right;
     HG 6.40 in full: H as the gate, split only when the prequential shadow gain G_v clears 2 block-SE and MDL.
   Subtree stall (growth-spec phase 2 as written) is NOT a league arm: by 6.40 (1) it is the control until ~v300,

   the 6.40 gate at 9M own examples after their leaves settle, ~v100-v200 at ~300k positions a cycle), about

   -45 to 0 at ply 2 (the busy DAG; 4 root copies per seed by v80). H minus C: 2-4 router splits per seed at

   rules: HG fires and is 20 or more over C at both plies: the first measured routing benefit, replicate. H is 20
   or more over C while HG is not: G_v is too strict (specialisation after the split is real) and the shadows
   should be judged over a longer life. H is at or below C (as predicted): the shadow gate is vindicated and
   routers are not where the gain is on these leaves. HG's refusals with H's losses together are the expected
   result; they close the router question at ply 1 until the leaves change (S7).

counts (4262 for a probit leaf split; bar 0.0106 at n 3M, 0.0032 at 10M, 0.0017 at 20M); section 3's "~0.004" was
an illustration with leaves only and is withdrawn. The bar is meant to refuse gains that never carried into play
(under ~0.004 nats, 6.29), and the smoke run shows it doing so (leaf 1's 0.0022 undone, the 0.041 and 0.059 kept).
(2) Carry n: add a per-node lifetime counter of examples routed through the node (never reset by an undo; a new

semantics for identity. A retry is then judged on the region's evidence, not the shadow's forgetting. (3) Accepted.

6.42 (21:40, 11-11). The offline discriminator (offline-ply/summary.txt) against 6.35: g1 0.088-0.095, g2 0.049-
0.058, g2/g1 0.58 +-0.05 over two training seeds; calibrated transfers P1-tree-on-P2 0.53, P2-tree-on-P1 0.84;
depth alone (P2x) keeps 0.56, players alone (P1x) 0.63; 2/3 of g in the lowest-material leaves at both plies (43%
of positions at ply 1, 9-19% at ply 2); winner-loss g 0.012-0.015 at both plies. Verdict between H1 and H2,
toward H2; my magnitudes were wrong by 8-15x (6.36 interim), my depth-only claim failed (P1x 0.032 below P1), and
the ratio was wrong (0.15-0.4 against 0.58). The fork's reading stands; mine follows.
(1) Interpretation. Ply-2 games hold more than half the structure, and the ply-1 tree's correction is half a
    correction for ply-2 outcomes: the field's play does not remove the partition's value, it halves it. So the
    league's ply-2 tie (-3 +-18 after 400 cycles, still closing) is not explained by the data; the edge is lost
    between log-loss and Elo, in one of two places:
    (a) WHAT PLAY WEIGHTS. At ply 2 the gain sits in deep endgames that hold 9-19% of positions, and the winner-loss
        gain (the measure nearest to what move ranking uses) is the same at both plies (0.013-0.015) while the Elo
        differs by 100: so neither full nor winner log-loss is the quantity that becomes Elo, and a gain that is
        calibration in already-decided positions buys no moves. If this is it, no training-loop change recovers the
        edge at ply 2.
    (b) TRACKING LAG. The league's trees train on a moving field (the ply-2 flat gained 122 Elo between v241 and
        v399; crownings ran to 360), and a leaf on a third or a ninth of the stream follows a moving target 3-9x
        more slowly than a flat on all of it; the tree's offline edge is paid back as lag until the field settles,
        which is why it was "still closing" at v399 and why P2's trees split later (v102, or not by v108). If
        this is it, the edge appears once the field plateaus and longer runs or fewer leaves (arm 1) recover it.
    The single test that separates them, minutes of compute: put the offline pairs INTO THE ARENA. The P2-trained
    F-P and T-P-Ne (one pass, 21.6M positions, fixed-player games, no drift) read cold at T 0.02 at ply 2 (and
    ply 1), with the P1 pair at ply 1 as the method's calibration (if the P1 offline tree does not show ~60-110
    over its flat, one-pass offline training itself underperforms the league and the test is void). Prediction:
    P1 pair, tree 60-110 over flat at ply 1; P2 pair, tree 20-60 over flat at ply 2 (centre 40), i.e. (b): the
    offline edge converts when there is no drift. Under (a) the P2 pair is within 20. A ply-3 read is optional.
(2) Programme. Stage 1: read as planned; base-probit-p2 stops at its floor 600 by the plateau rule, and the
    v300/v600 pair is the slope that (b) predicts positive and (a) flat; base-lr-p2's read is the LR version of
    the same question. Stage 2: arm 1 (limit 1 against 4 at ply 2) is worth its field ONLY if the arena says (b);
    then it is the direct test of lag (3 leaves follow the field 3x faster than 9) and should run next at ply 2.
    Under (a) drop every ply-2 split-parameter arm and move the ply-2 work to what play weights: the training
    objective (a loss weighted toward undecided positions, which the user may or may not allow) or the leaves'
    features (S7). limit-p1 stays the low-priority filler it was. The offline finding to carry into every later
    offline contrast: two training seeds differ by 3-4 held-out SEs, so seeds set the error, not the SE.
(3) Split criteria. The gain's concentration in the lowest-material leaf (lowest LOSS, highest GAIN) says grow's
    scheduler priority is wrong: it starts the trial at the leaf whose loss most exceeds the model's (the
    middlegame), which is where the gain is not. A split's worth is not the leaf's loss. Fix for the next grow
    build, as a one-factor arm at ply 1 later (--grow-priority {excess | size | gain}): size (largest leaf first,
    where the evidence is; the endgame leaf is the largest at ply 1) is the cheap exact proxy; "gain" would need
    a first-order split statistic. Not a change for grow-p1 now (it runs as built) and not an arm for router-p1
    (its routers are judged by G_v, which measures gain directly); grow-p1's own trial record will show whether
    excess-first ever reaches the endgame leaf.

6.43 (22:05, 11-11). offline-pairs-cold: P1 tree minus P1 flat 77 / 71 at plies 1-2 (the method holds); P2 tree
minus P2 flat -35 / -24; P2 flat minus P1 flat -222 / 99; P2 tree minus P1 tree -333 / 4. So (a), or worse: on a
fixed pool at equal data the ply-2-trained tree is no better than its flat in ply-2 play despite g2 0.05 nats,
and ply-2 data lifts the flat ~100 at ply 2 while the tree's ply-2 play is the same trained on P1 or P2.
(1) Why. Two facts together: the P2 flat is still DATA-LIMITED at 21.6M (in the league too it gained 122 at ply 2
    between v241 and v399 while the ply-1 flat had plateaued by v226: the ply-2 value function is slower to
    learn), and the tree's log-loss gain sits in decided deep endgames (57-68% of g2 in 9-19% of positions, 0.38
    nats a position there) where calibration buys no moves. The tree's middlegame leaves, where moves are decided,
    each train on a third to a ninth of a stream the flat has not finished learning from, so they lag the flat
    by the data they do not see, and the partition's endgame calibration cannot pay for it in play. At ply 1 the
    flat saturates early, the leaves' shares suffice, and the partition's gain is net positive. Fragmentation and
    placement are both there, but the binding term is data hunger at ply 2. Cheapest checks, no games: (i) per-
    leaf WINNER loss and rank-test of P2 tree against P2 flat on P2 held-out: prediction, the tree is behind the
    flat in the middlegame leaves (npm > 20) by winner loss and rank, and its endgame gain is full-loss only;
    (ii) the flat's data curve: train F-P on the first 36 and 12 rounds of P2 (7.2M, 2.4M: a level-1 and level-2
    leaf's share) and of P1, score held-out and, if wanted, one small arena: prediction, at ply 2 the 7.2M flat is
    0.01-0.02 nats and 30-60 Elo behind the 21.6M flat while at ply 1 the gap is under 0.005 and 15. That pair of
    numbers is the mechanism, and (ii) is the one to show the user.
(2) The ply-2 screen. Confirmed: every ply-2 split-PARAMETER arm (limit, threshold, gate, routers) is dropped;
    they re-divide a stream the flat still needs whole. limit-p2 is answered by (ii) at a hundredth of the cost.
    Stage 1 runs to its reads (the v300/v600 slope is now predicted flat, within 15, for both kinds). What
    replaces them at ply 2: an architecture that keeps the flat's data efficiency and adds the partition's
    correction, i.e. leaves as a shared trunk plus a per-leaf residual (the trunk trains on every example, the
    residual on the leaf's share, shrunk toward zero), which the code already has in a form (residual leaves and
    their shadows, bdt.rs 180; resid-src). Arm: T-P-Ne with residual leaves x2 against F-P x2 at ply 2 (and the
    same field at ply 1 to show it keeps the ply-1 gain), one factor, after the user's ruling under (3). Until
    then the honest line is: at ply 2 the flat is the right model for these features, and the way forward is
    more data per parameter, not more parameters.
(3) Against the constraints (self-play only, zero knowledge, no hand features; model-driven changes allowed;
    captures declined as knowledge):
    - Objective weighting by the model's OWN uncertainty (weight a position by p(1-p) of the current prediction,
      or train the trial on winner loss, an existing option): PASSES, like eval-swing extensions: it uses nothing
      but the model's output. Weighting by phase, material or move type would NOT pass.
    - Residual (trunk + leaf) leaves: PASSES as architecture; it is parameter sharing with a trunk, not the
      sharing of leaves between routers that the user excluded on 11-11, but it should be put to them as such.
    - S7 pair features (every product of two existing indicators as a candidate, chosen by a first-order
      statistic of the data, zero-initialised, shrunk, dropped when they stay near zero): PASSES the letter (no
      chess concept enters; only interactions of the inputs the model already has, selected by the labels) and
      is unlike captures, which name a move type; it is the one option that raises what a leaf can represent,
      and the user should rule on it knowing it is learned feature construction.
    - Anything that selects positions or features by chess terms (phase bands, piece counts, captures): fails.

(3) Read method. BT measures strength against the field's mix, which is right for the global league (the field
    is the judge) and wrong for a one-factor contrast whenever the field holds players from different training
    fields or plies, where the mix is non-transitive (the P2 flat exploits ply-1 play by ~100 more than the P2

    P 79 came from BT in a mixed-ply field; re-read head-to-head before citing them) and any read mixing versions

6.45 (22:55, 11-11). offline-drift: the pair trained on P2x rounds 0-53 then P2 rounds 54-107 against the pair
trained on P2 0-107, head-to-head at ply 2: drift tree against drift flat -33 (lag erases the tree's edge, as (b)
said); but drift flat over P2 flat 244 [199, 299], drift tree over P2 tree 76, at equal positions; at ply 1 the
same swap is worth 22. Both pairs END on the same data (P2 rounds 54-107), so recency and ordering are out; the
whole effect is what P2x's first half contains that P2's first half does not.
(1) Interpretation: COVERAGE, not strength. P2's players are the strongest ply-2 evaluators we have and two near-
    twins of two kinds at T 0.1, so their games are narrow: the same lines, positions both sides play well, few
    of the bad positions a 2-ply search must evaluate (every child and grandchild, most of them moves no strong
    player makes). P2x's players are weaker and different, so their games reach those positions and label them.
    An evaluator at depth k is queried OFF-POLICY, on positions the generating players never reach, and the
    deeper the search the larger that set; training data from the best players at the same depth covers it
    least. That also rereads the "data hunger" (188 Elo from r36 to r108 at ply 2): a narrow pool adds coverage a
    little with every game, so the curve stays steep. A calibration or base-rate artefact is unlikely at T 0.02
    (near-argmax, ranking decides) and would not be ply-2-only. Cheapest checks, in order: (i) position
    uniqueness per pool (distinct position hashes per 1M positions; duplicate whole games; distinct openings to
    move 10) for P1, P1x, P2, P2x: prediction, P2 lowest by a wide margin, P2x and P1x near P1; (ii) the P2x-ONLY
    pair (21.6M of P2x, which is on disk) against the P2 pair at ply 2: coverage predicts P2x-only flat over P2
    flat by 100-250, i.e. weaker players at ply 2 make better ply-2 training data than the strongest; (iii) the
    50/50 interleave (strongest if coverage, within 30 of the drift flat) and the reversed order P2 then P2x
    (within 50 of the drift flat; a large drop would revive recency). (ii) is the one to show the user.
(2) The league at ply 2. A pure ply-2 field trains each line on its own narrow games, so the stage-1 fields have
    been measuring architectures in a data regime that is far from the best available; the user's thunderdome
    is, by this result, the right design for ply-2 data, and pure experiments understate ply-2 models by 100-250.
    Cleanest one-factor test (diversity-p2): the stage-1 probit recipes, F-P x2 and T-P-Ne x2 at ply 2, with the
    P2x players (G2's finals, frozen, ply-1-trained) added as four fixed reference players at ply 2 whose games
    enter everyone's training set; comparator: the same recipes in the pure fields (probit-late-p2, base-probit-
    p2), read head-to-head cold at equal versions (v300, v400) at ply 2. One factor: the references' games in the
    stream (the field effect is the factor here, by design). Prediction: diverse-field F-P over pure-field F-P by
    100-200 at ply 2 at v400; the tree's gain over its flat in the diverse field within 20 (the drift tree gained
    less than the flat: leaves absorb coverage more slowly). Cost ~2 CPU-h to v400. The references are frozen,
    not random (the user's 11-10 rule excluded random players); put it to the user as such. If they want purity
    kept, the G option with an Elo cutoff on global-league games is the fallback, but those are ply-1 games
    (P1-type, worse for ply 2 than P2's own by 99), so it tests the wrong thing.
(3) limit-p2: the drift test already shows lag erasing the tree's edge, and a pure ply-2 field is now a poor
    data regime; run diversity-p2 first and limit-p2 only inside the diverse regime, if the tree's gain is there
    to protect. router-p1 is unchanged: ply 1 is where the field's own games are adequate (the swap was worth 22
    there) and where the router question is testable.

6.46 (23:20, 11-11). (A) offline-order: sequential (P2x then P2) flat 227 over the P2-only flat, replicating 244;
reversed (P2 then P2x) -63, and -360 against sequential; interleaved 186 (105 below sequential); trees over their
own flats: P2-only 100, interleaved 49, sequential -47, reversed -38. My "reversed within 50" failed by 300.
(i) Interpretation. The one-pass probit learner is RECENCY-WEIGHTED: its state is dominated by the last few
    million examples (SGD with a fixed step forgets at the step's rate; NB counts with forget 1 do not, which is
    why the routers are not the issue). So what the stream ENDS on sets the model: ending on P2x (weaker, broader
    games) after P2 is 360 worse than the reverse; broad coverage followed by precise play (sequential) or mixed
    throughout (interleaved) is best, with sequential ahead by 105 because its last examples are all precise.
    Coverage and recency are both real: coverage says the stream must contain the off-policy positions a 2-ply
    search asks about; recency says they must keep arriving, or arrive before the precise play that the model
    should end on. Trees beat their flats on STATIONARY streams (100, 49) and lose under a switch (-47, -38): a
    leaf on a third of the stream has a memory three times longer in time, so under a switch it is staler than
    the flat for three times as long, and on a stationary stream staleness costs nothing. That is (b), stated in
    the learner's own terms: lag = recency x share. Recency is tunable in the leaf learner (the step size or a
    decay, not --forget-factor, which is the NB routers'), and the stationary mix is the better fix than a longer
    memory, which would also slow learning.
    League design: every cycle's training set is the newest games only, so a pure field always ENDS on its
    newest, narrowest games, and the models track a narrowing stream; base-lr-p2's F-L v599 losing to its own
    v300 by 51 at ply 2 (B) is what that looks like late in a pure field. Design: each cycle's set = the cycle's
    games plus a fixed-share REPLAY of older own-field games (uniform over cycles up to t - 50) plus other-field
    or reference games at a fixed share, so the stream is stationary and broad (the interleaved regime, 186). Two
    one-factor arms, each one field at ply 2 against the pure stage-1 fields head-to-head at equal versions:
    replay-p2 (own older games at 50% of each cycle's set; no new players, no purity issue) and diversity-p2 as
    6.45 (frozen P2x references' games, which arrive every cycle, i.e. interleaved). Prediction: replay-p2 F-P
    over pure F-P by 60-150 at ply 2 at v400; diversity-p2 100-200 (unchanged); the tree's gain over its flat in
    both 20-60 (stationary streams restore it). Run replay-p2 first: it needs no ruling from the user.
(ii) B. base-lr-p2: LR cluster tree over F-L 27 / 20 / -26 at v599 (the first non-negative ply-2 tree contrast
    in a league, within its +-28), LR DAG (unsplit, the error tree) 0 / -9 / -48: consistent with the stationary-
    stream reading (the LR field drifted less: F-L v599 against v300 -7 / -51 / 57). The -51 is the finding:
    reads must take the BEST version by head-to-head, not the last, and a pure field past its coverage peak is
    not a plateau but a decline; check base-probit-p2's F-P v600 against v300 the same way. stall-p1: no
    difference (-8 / 16, -16 / -23, within noise, as predicted), the stall rule spends its splits 3x later; the
    rule is harmless and not a gain; grow-p1 is the test that matters for growth. Nothing else changes.
(iii) C, grow2 rulings. (1) Make the children guard share-based: the router is judged when children holding at
    least 90% of its lifetime examples have closed a window (the subtree weighting); a 0.7% child never closes
    one and must not block. (2) Not a bug, and not readable either: the cluster shadows pay the fragmentation
    cost (each copy sees a third of R's stream, a moving third while Q_v re-clusters) and a falling G measures
    that cost, which a real split would also pay but transiently. Add a NULL shadow set with the same shape
    (partition by the hash of the position, as the cluster warm-up does; copies of R; same updates) and make the
    statistic G_real - G_null, paired by block, with its block SE; split iff > 2 SE and > the MDL bar; log both.
    HG's refusals mean nothing until the null exists, so do not launch HG without it. (3) k = (m - 1) d_R: the
    split replaces one label-fitted router by m (the cluster top router is label-free and does not count), so
    5,112 for B 3, which is --grow-mdl 0.5 on the current 10,224; bar 0.0041 at 10M, 0.0137 at 3M. Launch
    router-p1 after (1)-(3); (1) and (3) are a line each, (2) is a second shadow set reusing the warm-up hash.
    No router credits is fine for router-p1 (all arms at --router-split-limit 4; the budget is not the factor).

6.47 (23:50, 11-11). router-p1 live (grow3, seed 45): the triggers' first logs.
(1) Stall is drift: router 0's routing error RISES at every close (2.61 -> 3.12; 2.96 -> 3.53) and grow-p1's leaf
    0 moves 0.70-0.78 between windows against block SEs of 0.001-0.018: the stream moves, the learner tracks it,
    and a window-to-window comparison measures the stream. Confirmed, and it is my error: 6.37's stagnation test
    assumed the stationary pools, where it was right, and a league field is never stationary (6.46). The paired
    snapshot statistic (frozen copy from the previous close against the online model on the same window) cancels
    drift, but under drift it has a FLOOR: the online model always beats a one-window-old copy by the tracking
    gain, so a node in a moving field never reads stagnant and the gate blocks forever. Ruling: no stagnation
    gate in league fields. Eligibility = age (three closed own windows) plus credits; the keep test is the trial,
    which is paired and sound; the paired snapshot gain is LOGGED and used as the scheduling PRIORITY (trial the
    leaf with the smallest learning gain first: most stagnant first), which keeps the user's "wait for
    stagnation" as an order rather than a gate that drift cannot satisfy. Same for routers.
(2) H cannot gate: correct. The routed child's realised loss minus the least realised loss is a minimum over B
    noisy outcomes and is positive for identical children; centring it needs a null that only the shadow pair
    provides, and an expected-loss version is a min over predictions with the same bias. H is withdrawn as a
    gate (logged only); the sound router statistic is G_real - G_null, as built. Shadows run from age one window
    at every router (cost 2m NB router updates per example at the router; fine at 9-13 routers).
(3) G_real - G_null turning negative (+0.0006, +0.0011, -0.0009, -0.0024, -0.0043, -0.0035; G_null within
    +-0.002 of 0 throughout) is the mechanism, not the statistic: the cluster partition is phase-like and
    correlated with the routing, so each cluster copy sees a biased subset of the routing targets and loses the
    counts for the other children; when the stream moves, the specialised copies cannot follow (lag x share
    again, now in the routers), while the null copies keep balanced counts. HG's refusal is a finding: on this
    field a router specialised by partition tracks worse than the shared one. The once-fitted Q_v is a secondary
    deviation from the spec (online hard EM was specified); refitting would change the magnitude, not the sign.
(a) router-p1 runs on (no stop without the user). Reading: C control; Z drift; H is a SCHEDULE arm (cluster splits
    when children holding 90% of the examples are ready, 4 per seed by ~v100-v150, the first at 11.0M), which
    still asks whether cluster router splits at a steady rate help in play; HG is G-only and refuses, so its
    lines are C's twins (read as noise, +-10). Predictions unchanged: H minus C -20 to 10 at ply 1, -30 to 10 at
    ply 2; Z minus C -10 to 10 / -45 to 0; HG = C. Extra readout: at H's split routers, the logged G_real - G_null
    in hindsight; if H sits within 10 of C at both plies while G refused, G is conservative in play terms; if H
    loses 20 or more, G was right. Either way the router question at ply 1 closes with this run.
(b) grow4 (leaves and routers): remove the stagnation gate (age three own windows plus credits); log the paired
    snapshot gain per window (snapshot at each close; both scored prequentially on each example; mean and block
    SE) and use it as the trial priority, smallest first; routers: H logged, not gating; shadows from age one;
    Q_v refit online. One-factor test: grow-p1b (grow4 against grow1 at ply 1, F-P x2, grow1 x2, grow4 x2, floor
    300) ONLY if grow-p1's trial record shows the stall gate blocked or misplaced trials; prediction, grow4 trials
    earlier and more often, same Elo within 10, 9-13 leaves at v300. A centred-H router-p1 copy is not worth
    building: H cannot be centred without the shadow pair, and HG without the H gate behaves as HG did (H always
    passed, so the shadows always ran). Wait for router-p1's read.
(c) base-probit-p2 read: beyond F-P v600 against v300, (i) tree and DAG against F-P head-to-head at v300 and
    v600 at ply 2, the lag prediction being that the gap opens after the crownings stop (~v360); (ii) DAG against
    cluster tree at ply 2 (the busy-DAG cost, both z 20 seeds split 4 times by v310); (iii) each line's v300 /

6.48 (00:20, 11-12). grow-p1 read: grow minus current -11 / 21 at v150, -3 / -22 at v399 (head-to-head, +-28);
grow 11-13 leaves against 9; 27 trials, 11 kept, 16 undone, and EVERY undone trial passed the z bar (z 3-10,
gains 0.0024-0.0068) and failed only the MDL bar (0.0055-0.0081 at n 4-6M); credits sat at the cap from ~20M.
Both trees gained 108-122 at ply 1 from v150 to v399 while F-P stood still, long after the current rule's last
split (v51): the leaves' late learning, not growth, carries the ply-1 gain (6.32 again).
Ruling on the bar: the coordinator is right, it double-counts, and it is miscalibrated besides. The trial is
prequential (each example scored before either model trains on it; children start as the parent's copies), and
prequential code length is two-part MDL asymptotically (Dawid, Rissanen): the branch's prequential loss over the
trial window already includes the regret of learning its extra parameters, so "split beats shadow at 2 block-SE"
IS the MDL test, and k ln n / 2n on top charges the parametric cost a second time, amortised over the past n
when a kept split's gain accrues on every future example. Miscalibrated because the ply-1 trees' own gains came
from splits worth 0.004-0.006 nats each (the league's revision logs), exactly the gains the bar refused at n
4-6M. The bar goes: --grow-mdl 0, with an explicit size margin --grow-min-gain 0.001 nats (about the 2-SE bar
at W 1M; it stops near-zero splits from accumulating depth and nodes). Credits then bound the size: no refunds
for kept splits, so kept splits <= N / D, i.e. 16 by 80M at D 5M (33 leaves, 49 nodes) and 24 by v600 (73
nodes, past hybrid15's 64-node fixed point, exact but slower); D 10M keeps 600 cycles under 64 nodes and is the
lean league setting once the bar is off. Back-off: keep the doubling for undone trials (with the bar off most
retries pass at the z bar, so it rarely binds).
Stall gate: it delayed (33-42 credit-waits) but did not bind (credits at cap); the bar did. So the bar goes
first. grow-p1b: one ply-1 field, two one-factor arms against grow1: F-P x2, grow1 x2, A = grow1 with the bar
off (flag only; launch now) x2, B = grow4 (no stagnation gate, age plus credits, snapshot-gain priority) x2 if
grow4 lands before launch, else B in the next field. Floor 300, cap 400, head-to-head reads at v150 and the
final, plies 1-2, plus the per-line split record against positions. Predictions: A keeps 20-30 splits per seed
by v399 (41-61 leaves; nodes pass 64 at ~v300, log it), A minus grow1 -15 to 10 at ply 1 and -30 to 0 at ply 2
(more leaves still buy nothing at ply 1 on these features; fragmentation lag at ply 2), A over F-P 80-120 at ply
1; B trials earlier, 12-15 leaves, within 10 of grow1 at both plies. Rule: A within 10 of grow1 at both plies
with 40 or more leaves says growth is governed by the z bar and D alone (size is the user's choice through D);
A 20 or more below at ply 2 says a margin is needed, set --grow-min-gain at the gain level below which A's
kept splits sit (probably 0.003). Per-ply read arenas: agreed; cross-ply games serve only the parity readout of
6.31 b, which is not a verdict, so drop them from reads unless parity is the question.

references), then the eight screened at ply 2 inside whichever regime wins; no split-parameter arms in pure
fields (6.43 (2) stands as corrected by 6.44: limit-p2 only inside the better regime, if a tree gain exists to
protect). replay-p2's read decides the plan as follows. (1) Replay flat 60 or more over the pure flat at ply 2
and the replay tree 20 or more over its flat: the regime restores both coverage and the tree's gain; the ply-2
screen moves into replay fields, and the share (0.25 against 0.5) is the one follow-up factor. (2) Replay flat 60
or more over pure but the tree within 20 of its flat: coverage restored, lag not; the tree needs a longer
window or the diversity regime; run diversity-p2 next. (3) Replay flat within 30 of pure: own older games are
the same narrow lines (same players, weaker), so own-field replay does not buy coverage; diversity-p2 (other
players) is the only route, and it needs the user. (4) Replay flat 30 or more BELOW pure: recency bites the
other way (each cycle ends half on stale games); lower the share to 0.25 or weight replay by age before judging
the regime. My prediction remains (1) with the flat at 60-150 and the trees over their flats by 20-60.

6.50 (23:45, 11-11). grow-p1b (v352): no-bar A minus grow1 -13 / 40 at v150, -2 / 19 at v352 (+-28); A over F-P
80 / 48, grow1 over F-P 115 / 99; A kept 11 of 11 trials (25 leaves), grow1 5-6 of 11 (13-15). The bar goes, as

needs: 25 leaves cost nothing against 13-15 at either ply (and ply 1 is inside my band, -2 against -15..10).
(1) A's 40 at v150 at ply 2: one version, one pairing, inside a non-transitive triangle: no weight on its own,
    and v352 gave 19. Its direction corrects my band, though: -30..0 at ply 2 assumed fragmentation lag, but lag
    needs a MOVING stream, and a ply-1 field's flat has plateaued by v150-v226, so extra leaves there are the
    stationary case of the offline pools, where more leaves cost nothing (error-growth-p1 said the same: 17
    leaves -3 at ply 2). Fragmentation lag is a ply-2-TRAINING phenomenon; it does not appear in ply-2 READS of
    ply-1 models. Band for A at ply 2 revised to -10..30; whether more leaves help at ply 2 reads stays open.
(2) B's prediction for the no-bar regime (grow-p1c): credits now bind outright (every trial is kept at the z
    bar), so B differs from A only in WHEN a credit is spent and on WHICH leaf: B spends each credit on arrival
    (age gate, several candidates), A waits for the stall gate (A's record had the "no candidate" waits). So B:

    A's ~11-12 (23-25); leaves at v300 (~60M) B 23-27, A 19-23; undone 0-2 in each; B's priority goes to the most

    ply 1, where extra leaves have bought nothing in three runs.
(3) D as the size knob, for the user. With the bar off every credit becomes a kept split at the z bar, so size
    follows the data directly: leaves ~ 1 + 2 N / D, i.e. at D 5M about 25-33 leaves per 80M positions (a 400-
    cycle ply-1 field) and 64 nodes (hybrid15's fixed-point limit; exact but slower above it) at ~105M; at D 10M
    half the leaves and 64 nodes at ~210M. So the choice to present is D 5M against 10M as "how many leaves per
    unit of data", with 10M the lean league setting (a global-league line at ~100k positions a cycle splits every
