    (d) the honest expectation: at ply 1 growth is free but has bought no Elo in three runs (grow -3, A -2 against
    the capped rule); its value is for leaves with more to learn (a broad ply-2 regime, or S7), so the league
    adopts it for growth's sake, not for strength now. W 1M, C 2 and z 2 stay as they are.

6.51 (00:15, 11-12). replay-p2 (own older games at share 0.5, window 50, shuffled into each cycle): replay minus
pure F-P 38 / -123 / -1 at v300 and 37 / -64 / -5 at v399; replay tree minus pure tree 27 / -131 / -47 and 79 /
-42 / -30. My prediction (flat 60-150 over pure at ply 2) failed by ~200, and the stated mechanism cannot be the
cause: the replayed games are shuffled with the fresh ones, and pure lag over a 25-cycle mean age is worth ~6.
Diagnosis. The signature is the one 6.30's theorem-level candidate predicts: the evaluator estimates P(outcome |
position, play of depth k), and WEAKER play has the blunder structure of SHALLOWER play. Own-field replay feeds
the lineage its own earlier play, which for the first 50-100 cycles is near-random (an untrained evaluator at
depth 2 plays like depth 0) and thereafter is weaker than the current version; the trained value function is
pulled toward the shallow-play function: better at ply 1 (+37-79, observed), worse at ply 2 (observed), near
zero at ply 3 (the other parity, observed), and the deficit closes as the pool matures (-123 to -64). P2x helped
offline (+186-244) because its games are depth-2 play by DIFFERENT fixed evaluators: new coverage of positions
the lineage never visits, with no from-scratch phase. So the two experiments say the same thing: coverage must
come from OTHER players, and anything fed back must pass a quality floor. Candidate (a) is this, stated as an
off-policy label effect; (b) compounds it (a worse field plays worse games; the deficit closes only as fast as
the field improves); (c) is unlikely (offline one-pass on 21.6M was fine with twice the positions). One check
without compute: what label a replayed game trains on (if any stored quantity depends on the version that
played, replay injects stale targets as well). Cheap reads: replay v542 against replay v399 at ply 2 (prediction
20-50: still recovering) and against pure v399 (prediction -40 to 0), ~4 CPU-min; and from cycles.csv the
rating gap between each cycle's current version and the replayed games' versions, which should be largest in
the first 100 cycles and track the deficit.
Next ply-2 step. Own-field replay at share 0.5 is closed as a route; a share of 0.25 only halves a pull in the
wrong direction, and age-weighting does not remove it. The one replay variant worth a field is an ELO-WINDOWED
replay (replay only games whose players were rated within 30 of the current version when played, the global
league's own rule), after diversity-p2, not before. diversity-p2 (frozen P2x references, the measured +186-244
regime) is the next step and needs the user's ruling on frozen reference players; put it to them with this
result: coverage from other players helps by ~200 at ply 2, coverage from one's own past hurts by 60-130. The
user's thunderdome design (many players, the Elo-window training rule) is exactly the regime these two results
point at, which is the strongest argument yet for the next global league running at ply 2.
6.49's plan stands with the regime named: the eight are screened at ply 2 inside a mixed-player field with a
quality floor, i.e. diversity-p2's regime or the global league itself, not in pure fields and not under own
replay. Prediction for diversity-p2 unchanged (F-P 100-200 over pure at ply 2 at v400; trees over their flats
20-60) and the replay result adds one: the references' games must not include the lineage's own early play.

6.51 note (00:40, 11-12). Checks: a replayed game carries only the outcome and the moves (hard link to the
original), so the only stale thing is the play; the diagnosis stands as "weaker own play, shallower value
function". Late read at ply 2: replay v542 over replay v399 F-P 7, tree 36 (my 20-50: flat under); replay v542
against pure v399 F-P 20, tree -20 (my -40..0: flat over); replay v399 against pure v399 repeated -42 / -74,
pooled about -53 / -58. So replay reaches the pure run's v399 level around v542, ~140 cycles later, and the
replay flat has nearly stopped improving by v542 (7 per 143 cycles against pure's 24 per 150): the field is at
a lower-reaching, slower equilibrium, the compounding of 6.51 (b). No rating-gap proxy is needed: the delay
itself is the measurement (a 140-cycle lag at a share of 0.5 and window 50), and a proxy from the versions'
cold ratings would cost an arena to say the same.
For the user, one addition to the framing: the two results also say what the global league's Elo window is
FOR. Games from players at or above one's own level supply coverage without the shallow-play pull; games from
one's own weaker past supply the pull without new coverage. The window is a quality floor on the play, not on
the players, and a ply-2 global league with it is the regime the offline interleave (+186) and the P2x drift
run (+244) measured. diversity-p2 (frozen P2x references, their games every cycle) is the controlled version
of that regime and the test to run before the league commits to ply 2: prediction F-P 100-200 over the pure
F-P at ply 2 at v400, trees over their flats 20-60. If the user declines frozen references, the Elo-windowed
replay of 6.51 is the fallback, with a lower ceiling (own play never adds coverage).

6.53 (01:50, 11-12). diversity-p2 design rulings (the user approved the plan; frozen external references, the P2x
four at v314 playing at ply 2 with recreated own calibrations, never training or crowning).
(1) Volume: keep 12 rounds and read at BOTH equal positions and equal versions; do not cut rounds. Cutting to 3
    would reduce the lines' own line-line games to a quarter of the pure fields' (36 against 144 a cycle), which
    changes the lineage's self-play dynamics, a second factor; at 12 rounds the line-line volume equals pure's
    and the references add their games on top (56 a round: 12 line-line, 32 line-reference, 12 reference-
    reference). Equal positions is the comparison the offline curve demands (the diversity field reaches pure
    v399's positions at about v85 and v599's at about v130); equal versions is the number a league gets per
    cycle. One run gives both. Floor 300, cap 400 (about 6-8 CPU-h at 4.7x the games of a pure cycle).
(2) Train on all three kinds. Reference-reference games are the stationary P2x pool itself (what the offline
    interleave mixed in), line-reference games are the off-policy coverage with the line's own play on one side,
    and the question is exactly whether weaker OTHER play helps where weaker OWN play hurt. Log the shares.
(3) Agreed: self-games on, ply1_entries false, as in the pure fields.
(4) F-P x2 and T-P-Ne x2 as probit-late-p2 (limit 4, 1M, noise 0.03); seed 48, new: a twin seed buys nothing once
    cycle 1 differs and invites false twin readings. Comparators: probit-late-p2 (v399) and base-probit-p2's F-P
    (v599) for equal positions.
Reads (head-to-head, 600 games, +-28, ply 2 unless said) and predictions: equal positions, diversity F-P at ~v85
against pure F-P v399: 60-150; equal versions, v300 against pure v300: 120-220 (coverage plus 4.7x the data),
v399 against pure v399: 100-200; tree over its flat inside the diversity field at v300: 0-50 (the interleaved
offline tree gained 49, the sequential one lost 47); at ply 1 diversity F-P over pure F-P 0-40 (the offline swap
was worth 22 there); at ply 3 within 40. A diversity F-P within 30 of pure at equal positions would say the
offline +186-244 does not transfer to a league stream, and the replay result's "weaker play pulls shallow"
would then apply to other players' play too; that is the result that would send the next league back to ply 1.

6.53 correction (02:10, 11-12). The volume numbers were wrong: a field holds each line's current and champion,
so probit-late-p2 played 672 games a cycle (8 entries, 12 rounds), base-probit-p2 and grow-p1 1,584 (12
entries), and diversity-p2 at 12 rounds plays 1,584 (12 entries: 8 line entries plus 4 references; 56 line-line,
64 line-reference, 12 reference-reference a round), 2.36x probit-late-p2, exactly base-probit-p2's batch, and
with the lines' own line-line volume EQUAL to probit-late-p2's (672). So (1) of 6.53 holds with the right
reason: 12 rounds keeps own-play volume equal to the pure comparator and adds the references' games on top.
The user's rule (games per cycle equilibrated by rounds): R = max(1, round(T / (N (N - 1)))) with N the entries
that play (currents, champions, references including random ones). Recommended T = 1,584 (the stage-1 batch),
range 1,200-2,000. What it buys: games per entry 2T/N, so rating SE about 348 / sqrt(2T/N): 28 Elo at N 20, 21 at
N 12, 17 at N 8; the crowning test at 2 SE of the diff then needs 78 Elo at N 20, 61 at N 12, 50 at N 8, stable
within a factor 1.6 across field sizes where today it ranges from 50 to over 100; the training batch is T games
a cycle whatever the field; CPU per cycle is T games at the field's ply, so cost no longer grows with the
square of the field. T 672 would keep probit-late-p2's batch but halve every field's stability; the stage-1
fields already run at 1,584, so T 1,584 changes nothing that runs.
diversity-p2: keep 12 rounds (T 1,584); comparators base-probit-p2's F-P at equal versions (equal batch, equal
positions; caveat: that field held DAG and cluster lines) and probit-late-p2 at equal positions (diversity v169
= pure v399) and at equal versions. Predictions (head-to-head, 600 games, +-28, ply 2): diversity F-P over base-
probit-p2 F-P at v300 and v599: 100-200; over probit-late-p2 F-P at equal positions (v169 against v399): 60-150;
at equal versions (v300 against v300): 120-220; tree over its flat inside diversity at v300: 0-50; ply 1: 0-40;
ply 3 within 40. The null that matters: within 30 of base-probit-p2's F-P at equal batch.
Implementation: recompute R every cycle from the actual N (entries change with retirements and entries); round
half up, floor 1 (a field over 40 entries exceeds T at R 1, accepted), cap 48 (a 2-line field would need 132
rounds for T; at the cap it plays 576 and is logged as under T); keep double round-robins with colour
alternation inside a round; log N, R, games played and training positions per cycle; self-games stay scaled by
lines as now and are not part of T; random references' games count in T (they are played) but stay excluded
from training by the existing rule; replace min_rounds / rounds_per_cycle by T in specs, with the old keys
honoured for finished runs' reproducibility. Note for reads: with references in a field the lines' own-play
share of the training set is L(L-1)/(N(N-1)), 42% here, by design.

6.54 (02:40, 11-12). router-p1 (v299; head-to-head against C, +-28; ply 1 / ply 2): HG 0 router splits, 4 / 5 (C's

(3) H has no shadows, so there is nothing in hindsight; the HG numbers above are the hindsight for the whole

6.56 (03:30, 11-12). diversity-p2 partial, equal positions at ply 2 (diversity v182 = probit-late v399 at 34.7M;
v140 = v300): F-P -2 and 6 (my 60-150: wrong; inside the 30 null); capped tree -59 and -48 against the pure
tree; tree against its flat inside diversity -21 at v182 and -61 at v140.
First, what equal positions compares. A league's progress is per CYCLE, not per position: each cycle's games
come from the improved policy (the curriculum), and the positions of one cycle are correlated (same players,
same lines), so 2.19x positions a cycle is worth far less than 2.19x cycles; the fixed-pool data curve (188 Elo
from r36 to r108) does not transfer to within-cycle volume. diversity v182 has had 182 cycles of its own
improvement against pure's 399, and nulling against it says only that 58% foreign games plus 42% own are worth
about what 100% own games are, at equal positions and half the cycles. The informative reads are equal
VERSIONS (same cycles) and equal BATCH (base-probit-p2's F-P, same cycles and the same 1,584 games, the factor
being foreign reference games in place of own-field sibling games); the main read has both.
(a) The references are ply-1-TRAINED, so their games at ply 2 are depth-2 play with shallow evaluators; offline
    those very games lifted the P2 flat by 186-244 and the tree by 49 over its flat, so the games are not
    poison in themselves. What differs in the league is WHO is stronger: offline the P2x games were mixed with
    STRONG narrow play (the finals'), which lacked coverage; in a from-scratch field the lines' early play is
    weak and already broad, so the references add little coverage early, and once the lines pass the references
    the reference games are weaker foreign play for the rest of the run, which by the depth-k reading is the
    replay pull from outside (weaker play, shallower value function). Prediction: the lines passed the
    references in-run between v60 and v150 (the references are P2x-level, 150-250 below probit-late's finals);
    check ratings.csv for the crossing. If the crossing was early, diversity-p2 tested "train on weaker foreign
    play for 250 cycles", which is not the regime the user's league has: the Elo window (games of players at or
    above own Elo - 30) drops the reference games exactly when they turn weaker. The test of the user's regime
    is diversity-p2b: the same field with the window as the one factor (the references' games enter training
    only while the references are rated within 30 of, or above, the line). Prediction for it: F-P over diversity-
    p2's F-P 30-100 at equal versions at ply 2, the gain made early and kept.
(b) The tree pays more because error routing partitions on prediction error, and with two populations of
    games the largest error structure is the SOURCE (positions typical of reference play against line play,
    with different outcome statistics), so leaves specialise by source rather than by position type, which is
    worthless against pure opponents cold. Cheap check: route a sample of reference-game and line-game
    positions through the diversity tree and tabulate the source share per leaf; a leaf over 80% one source
    says the partition is by source. Prediction: at least two of nine leaves over 80% reference positions.
    Under the window (b) also fades, since the two populations stop coexisting once the lines are ahead.
(c) Before "back to ply 1": the equal-version reads (v300 against v300, v399 against v399, and diversity final
    against pure v599: the narrow-late-pool reading says the gain, if any, appears late), the crossing point
    from ratings.csv, the source-per-leaf table, and then diversity-p2b with the window if the crossing was
    early. The 6.53 rule is amended: equal positions cannot send the league anywhere; the window test can.

as replay's 37-79 did); ply 3 within 40. The user's regime (coverage from STRONGER foreign play under the

question before the league commits, 6-8 CPU-h; prediction: ACCELERATION, the lines reach pure's v300 level in

not the winner loss; it is the loss on the positions a search ranks between, which no current diagnostic
isolates; a rank test restricted to undecided positions (|p - 0.5| under 0.3 by the flat) would be the one to
build if this keeps recurring.

6.57 (04:15, 11-12). diversity-p2c design (coverage from STRONGER foreign play under the window).
1. References: base-probit-p2's v599 F-P x2 and T-P-Nc-merge-fresh x2 (the strongest ply-2 models on disk; the
   DAG lines play slower on the fixed-point path and are no stronger; the references' architecture is
   immaterial, their play is the data). Not probit-late's v399: 67 weaker, and the point is references the
   lines cannot pass within the run.
2. Window scope (a): a line trains on a reference game only while that reference is rated at or above the
   line's current Elo minus M; line-line games always train, as in every experiment. (b) would add a second
   factor. Since the references should stay above the lines all run, the window is expected to be INERT here,
   as it was in diversity-p2 after cycle 8; it is built so that the regime is the league's, and so that if a line
   does pass a reference the data changes as the league's would. Log every cycle in which the window excludes
   anything.
3. M = 30 (the global league's train.margin). Reference-reference games train while both references pass, i.e.
   all run: they are the stationary strong pool.
4. Lines: diversity-p2's (F-P x2, T-P-Ne x2, seed 48, games_per_cycle 1,584). Comparators: base-probit-p2's F-P
   at equal versions (equal batch, same cycles; the factor is foreign stronger games in place of sibling games),
   probit-late-p2 at equal versions (half batch), and diversity-p2 (same lines and seed; the window is inert in
   both runs after cycle 8, so that contrast is reference STRENGTH alone in practice).
5. Floor 300, cap 400, as diversity-p2 (about 75 min wall at 11 s a cycle); the ceiling claim is read at v400
   against bfp v450 (the nearest version read) and v300 against bfp v300.
6. Reads, per-ply arenas at plies 1 and 2 (no ply 3; parity is not a verdict): ply 2 at v150, v300 and the final
   against bfp (v300, v450), probit-late (v300, v399) and diversity-p2 (v300, v399); ply 1 at the final; the
   acceleration ladder: p2c F-P at v100 / v150 / v200 against probit-late F-P v300 at ply 2 (one small arena);
   the cycle at which any line passes any reference (none expected) and the per-cycle reference share of the
   batch (about 58-60% throughout).
Predictions (head-to-head, +-28, ply 2): acceleration, p2c F-P at v150 against probit-late F-P v300 0-60 and at
v200 20-80; p2c F-P against bfp at v300 -20 to 40 and at v400 against bfp v450 -40 to 20 (the ceiling within
30 of the pure field's); p2c F-P over diversity-p2's F-P at v300 20-80 (stronger references beat weaker); the
p2c tree against its flat -40 to 0 (the source partition again); ply 1 at the final, p2c F-P against pure
-20 to 30 (no shallow pull from strong ply-2 play). What would change the plan: p2c F-P 40 or more over bfp at
v400 says strong foreign play raises the ceiling and the league goes to ply 2 with the window as designed;
within 30 says the regime buys speed only, and the league's ply-2 question becomes one of compute, not of
strength; 40 or more below says strong narrow play starves coverage and the references must be broad (a mix of
strengths above the window), which is the thunderdome's own composition rather than a fixed reference set.

6.58 (04:40, 11-12). diversity-p2 main read (v300 / v314; +-28): at equal batch dfp minus bfp 6 / 15 at ply 2, 6 /
31 at ply 1, -5 at ply 3 (the 6.53 null; my 6.56 band of -80..-20 was wrong too); dfp minus pfp (half batch)
-10 / 44 at ply 2, 84 / 55 at ply 1, 74 at ply 3; the tree under its flat everywhere (-2 to -42). Two
predictions failed in opposite directions and the read is the null: 58% of the batch from 200-weaker frozen
foreign play is worth what own-field sibling games are worth, with no replay-style pull.
(a) Why no pull. Replay's deficit was largest at v300 (-123) and closing by v399 (-64): the shape of a pool that
    held the lineage's from-scratch garbage (untrained evaluators at depth 2 play like depth 0) for its first
    50-100 cycles and then aged out of it, not of a steady pull from play 50 cycles old. The references here
    are 200 below the lines but at a fixed, decent level (depth-2 search with a trained ply-1 evaluator) and were
    never garbage. And foreign play is uncorrelated with the lineage: it visits positions the lineage does not
    (coverage), where own past play visits the lineage's own lines (no coverage, staleness only). So the value
    of a training game is coverage times quality: own past play has no coverage and, early, no quality; fixed
    weaker foreign play has coverage and tolerable quality, netting to about the value of sibling games;
    stronger foreign play (p2c) is the open case. The batch did not offset anything: the null is at equal batch.
    Replay with a minimum source cycle (no games from before cycle ~100) would probably not have hurt, but the
    route stays closed: it cannot add coverage.
(b) diversity-p2c stands as designed; the comparator pair is now better than I had it: dfp = bfp at equal batch,
    so p2c against either isolates reference STRENGTH (the window inert in both runs). Bands reconciled: p2c
    F-P over bfp and over dfp at v300 0-60 (centre 25); at v400 against bfp v450 -30 to 30; the acceleration
    ladder as 6.57. Launch when the window build is verified.
(c) Yes, read the batch contrast from existing versions: pfp against bfp at v300 and v399, plies 1-3, one small
    arena per ply. Prediction: bfp over pfp 10-50 at ply 2 at v300 and 0-40 at v399; 40-90 at plies 1 and 3
    (implied by dfp = bfp and dfp over pfp). Caveat for the record: base-probit-p2's 1,584 games came from six
    lines of three kinds, so batch size is confounded with sibling composition; it is the read available, and
    a clean 4-line 1,584-against-720 field is not worth a run now that the standard is set. What the batch
    result would say: per-cycle progress scales with the batch at plies 1 and 3 and weakly at ply 2, i.e. at
    ply 2 the limit is the quality and coverage of the play, not its volume, which is the ply-2 story since 6.45.

6.58 note (04:55, 11-12). p2c's references are not stronger than the lines: on the pilot's zero the bfp v599
F-Ps rate 1661 / 1614 and the cluster trees 1511 / 1492, while diversity-p2's lines plateaued in-run at ~1650-
1690 by cycle 60. There is no ply-2 player on disk that a from-scratch ply-2 line does not reach within the run,
so "coverage from STRONGER foreign play" cannot be tested with fixed references; only the league itself makes
stronger players as it goes. Ruling: (i), launch as built. It is then the league's regime literally (references
at the lines' level, the window active and cutting them as they are passed), and its reads are informative
either way. Predictions (+-28, ply 2): the cluster references cut by cycle ~10, the F-P references between v100
and v300; p2c F-P against bfp at v300 within 30 (centre 10) and at v400 against bfp v450 within 30; the
acceleration ladder p2c v150 against pfp v300 0-40; the tree under its flat by 0-40. What each would mean: 30 or
more over bfp at v300 says window-mediated references help even at equal strength (the league's regime adds
strength); within 30 says the regime neither helps nor hurts at equal strength, so a ply-2 league's value over
a ply-2 field is its players and compute, not a data effect; 30 or more below says the window cut references
that were still stronger cold, which points at the real risk in the rule: the window judges on in-run ratings
from T 0.1 play, which dilute ranking edges (6.28 side note: 1-6 Elo at T 0.1 against 118 cold), so a line can
"pass" a reference in-run that still beats it cold. Log the cut cycles and, at the read, test each cut
reference against the line's version of that cycle cold at ply 2; if the reference wins cold by 30 or more, the
league's window needs the cold or pooled rating, not the cycle's. The main read changes nothing else in p2c.

6.59 (05:20, 11-12). grow-l-p1 (v299; +-28; ply 1 / ply 2): grow minus capped -25 / 48 at v150, -16 / 41 at v299
(my -10..10 / -10..20: ply 1 under, ply 2 over); capped minus F-L 38 / -101 and 21 / -38; grow minus F-L 17 /
-64 and 34 / -47. Trials: LR kept 4-5 of 14-15, 7-8 undone with NEGATIVE gains (z -1 to -5), kept gains ~0.0008
nats; probit on the same rule kept all 10-12 at 0.011-0.014. My 6.55 (d) (0.002-0.004 at z 3-7) was wrong by
3-5x, and the failure is not power: the LR children LOSE to the unsplit leaf within the window.
(a) Mechanism, from the learners (errdag4-src logreg.rs, probit.rs). LR is AdaGrad: step = rate x g / sqrt(sum
    of squared gradients), and forget(factor) DIVIDES those sums by the factor. The recipes run --forget-factor
    1, so at a split the children and the shadow inherit the parent's full AdaGrad sums, accumulated over tens
    of millions of examples: every copy learns at the parent's late-life step size, and the LR recipe has no
    split noise, so the children are exact copies. Under the field's drift the shadow then wins by arithmetic:
    the same tiny step with three times the updates tracks the moving target; a child on a third of the data
    falls behind it, and specialisation cannot show inside 1M examples. Probit is ADF: the split noise 0.03
    widens the children's variances (their step sizes), and tau^2 dynamics keep every weight's step alive, so
    the children adapt within the window and win. So the LR failure is in the learner's split mechanics, not
    in the rule: the children need a fresh step. The fix is one existing flag: --forget-factor 10 (main.rs's
    own default, which the recipes overrode) divides the AdaGrad sums at the split, raising the children's
    and the shadow's steps by sqrt(10); a split-only equivalent would be cleaner but needs a build. The capped
    LR tree has the same defect without a trial to expose it: its leaves learn at the parent's late-life step
    on a third of the data for the rest of the run, which fits the LR family's smaller and erratic gains.
(b) T-L-Ne-grow does not enter the six as built. Test the fix first, one factor, ply 1: grow-l-p1b, F-L x2,
    T-L-Ne-grow forget 1 x2 (known), T-L-Ne-grow forget 10 x2, same seed family, floor 300, cap 400, 2 CPU-h.
    Prediction: forget 10 keeps 8 or more of 12 trials with positive gains of 0.002-0.005 nats (z 3-8), 0-3
    undone, 17-25 leaves; forget 10 minus forget 1 0-30 at ply 1 and -20..20 at ply 2; forget 10 over F-L 30-60
    at ply 1. If it holds, T-L-Ne-grow (forget 10) takes the LR tree seat; if not (fewer than 6 kept, or 20 or
    more below forget 1 at ply 1), the six carry F-L alone on the LR side (five lines, or the capped LR tree as
    a fixed control), and the LR family's trees are set aside until the learner is revisited. W 2M is not the
    follow-up: the children lose, they are not under-measured.
(c) The capped LR tree's ply-2 reads across four ply-1 fields: -43 (lr-trees-p1), -17 (G6, cluster), 55 (error-
    dag-p1), -101 / -38 here: a spread of 150, beyond the +-40 of field and version noise. Something real: LR
    leaves with a frozen late-life step, each on a share of the stream, hold ply-1-field corrections that a
    ply-2 read exposes unevenly from field to field (the lag of (a), in a read one ply away). The probit error
    tree's ply-2 reads from ply-1 fields were 77, 52, 64, 69-70: stable. The LR tree is the fragile seat of the
    six at ply 2 whatever (b) finds, and the user should know it when the league's ply is chosen.

6.60 (05:45, 11-12). Batch read, bfp minus pfp at equal versions (v300 / v399): ply 2 -37 / -55 (my 10-50:
sign wrong), ply 1 83 / 47 (inside), ply 3 -17 / 68. base-probit-p2's F-P, with 2.2x the games from a six-line
field of three kinds, is the better ply-1 player and the WORSE ply-2 player than probit-late's F-P. Volume cannot
do that; the depth-k pull can: the sign pattern (up at ply 1, down at ply 2) is the signature of training on
WEAKER play (replay: 37-79 / -64..-123; diversity against pfp: 55-84 / -10..44; now 47-83 / -37..-55), and in
base-probit-p2 two thirds of the line-line games involve the cluster trees and DAGs, which at ply 2 were 10-50
weaker than the flats (6.49), while in probit-late the trees were level with the flat (no pull). So the
composition carries it, with volume as the carrier, and the user's 1,584 standard is not the problem: at ply 2
the QUALITY of the games in the batch relative to the trainee is, which is the Elo window's job. Two
consequences. (i) Every mixed-kind ply-2 field so far handicapped its flats by their weaker siblings' games;
6.49's "flat beats the trees by 10-50" was measured with the flat pulled down, and a windowed field would
likely widen it. Experiments that mix strengths at ply 2 should run with the window, or be read knowing this.
(ii) It also resolves the offline puzzle (+186-244 from P2x into P2, nothing in the league): the offline P2 pool
was games among four FIXED finals, the narrowest stream possible; a league's pure field drifts every cycle,
and that drift is itself coverage over time, so a league never has the narrowness the offline pool had. The
league result stands as the real one: at ply 2 no regime yet beats a pure field at equal versions, and games
from weaker players, own or foreign, pull toward shallow play by an amount that grows with the gap. For the
league at ply 2: the window is essential, not optional; the strongest line trains on its own games and its
equals', which is the pure regime; and the ply-2 ceiling of the field is set by the top's own play, since
stronger players do not exist to learn from. p2c's F-P references sit at the lines' level, so no pull: its
null prediction stands (the cluster references were cut at cycle 10, as predicted).

6.61 (06:10, 11-12). The sceptical check of 6.60 is right: with a free coverage term and +-40 of field noise the
"pull grows with the gap" account forbids nothing, and it already has a strike in the data: diversity-p2
(58% of the batch from 200-weaker foreign play) should then be the WORST case and is the null (dfp minus bfp
6 / 15 at ply 2), where siblings 10-50 weaker in ~40% of the batch (base) cost 37-55. I withdraw the gap
scaling and the coverage term. The account that fits the three results with no free term: harm comes from
weaker play IN THE TRAINEE'S OWN DISTRIBUTION, that is, worse play at the positions the trainee itself
reaches, which corrupts the labels where it is evaluated (own past: same lines, worse outcomes; siblings of
the same family on the same stream: the same); weaker play ELSEWHERE (foreign players, other openings and
styles) labels positions the trainee does not reach and is neutral or mildly useful. Replay (in-
distribution, harm), base's siblings (in-distribution, harm), diversity's references (out of distribution,
null) all fit without a coverage knob. What it forbids: weaker FOREIGN play harming at ply 2 (diversity-p2 is
the one data point, null; p2c cannot show it with the window on); and a flat-only field failing to beat base's
flat at ply 2.
(1) Existing data gives no clean test: within base-probit-p2 the sibling share is constant by construction
    (every cycle has the same round structure), and the F-P's progress against the trees' gap goes the wrong
    way for a pull (34 per 150 cycles while the gap was 40-50, 24 while it narrowed to 12) but is confounded
    with saturation; the cross-experiment regression has four points and two terms. Not decisive either way.
(2) Cheapest decisive run, no build: an F-P-ONLY ply-2 field, F-P x4, new seed, games_per_cycle 1,584 (8
    entries, 28 rounds), floor 300, cap 400, ~4 CPU-h; read at equal versions against bfp (same batch, weaker
    siblings removed: the composition factor) and pfp (720 games, level siblings: the volume factor), plies 1
    and 2, v300 and the final. Predictions: F-P x4 minus bfp 20-60 at ply 2 and -60 to -20 at ply 1 (the
    coupling: what the siblings' games cost at ply 2 they gave at ply 1); F-P x4 minus pfp within 30 at both
    plies (quality equal, volume 2.2x: little). The result that kills both quality accounts: F-P x4 within 15
    of bfp at ply 2 and 30 or more below pfp; then the harm is VOLUME per cycle at ply 2 (a one-pass learner
    stepping 2.2x per cycle on a stream that moves faster for it), and the 1,584 standard should drop to ~720
    for ply-2 fields. The windowed-siblings build (scope (b)) is the second test, only if F-P x4 supports
    composition; it would then tell whether the window recovers all of the 37-55.

6.62 (06:30, 11-12). grow-l-p1b (v347; +-28; ply 1 / ply 2): forget 10 minus forget 1 -24 / 30 at v150, -13 / 75
at v347 (my 0-30 / -20..20: ply 1 under, ply 2 far over); forget 10 over F-L 41 / 36, forget 1 over F-L 89 /
-28. Trials: forget 10 kept 6-7 of 12-15 (gains 0.0021-0.0022, inside my band), undone 5-9 (I said 0-3); forget
1 kept 4 of 17-18 (0.0009-0.0010, replicating grow-l-p1). The mechanism is real (gains doubled, negative undos
halved) and half-fixed: LR splits still fail 40-60% of trials where probit's fail none. By the rule (6 or more
kept, not 20 below at ply 1) T-L-Ne-grow with forget 10 takes the LR seat.
(a) The residual failure is structural, in the trial, not in AdaGrad alone. AdaGrad's step is rate / sqrt(sum
    of squared gradients); forget 10 divides the sums, a x3.2 step at the split that decays as the sums refill
    (a parent of 10-40M examples divided by 10 is a 1-4M history, so the boost lasts a few windows: enough).
    But split_leaf forgets the SHADOW too (grow4-src: the shadow is a forgotten copy), so the trial compares
    three boosted children on thirds of the stream with ONE boosted leaf on all of it, when the alternative to
    the split is the UNBOOSTED leaf as it would have continued. Under drift the boosted shadow with 3x the
    updates tracks best, and the children must beat it by specialisation alone: a bias against splits in both
    families, invisible in probit (forget 1 changes nothing there; the noise widens children only) and
    exposed in LR by the very fix. The next step is one line, not a larger factor: the shadow keeps the
    parent's sums (no forget; children only), so the trial asks the right question; forget 100 would boost the
    shadow as much as the children and destabilise early updates. Worth it for one seat? One line and one 2-
    CPU-h field (grow-l-p1c: forget 10 with an unforgotten shadow against forget 10 as built; prediction: kept
    9-12 of 12-15, undone 2-5, gains 0.002-0.004, Elo within 15 of forget 10 at both plies). If the user wants
    the LR seat as sound as the probit's, yes; otherwise forget 10 is the LR recipe and the build waits.
(b) Yes, it revises the fragility reading. The capped LR trees and the global league's LR lines ran forget 1,
    so their leaves learned at the parent's late-life step on a share of the stream for the whole run; forget
    10's 30 / 75 over forget 1 at ply 2 is the signature of that defect being lifted. Prediction: a capped T-L-
    Ne with forget 10 reads 20-60 over F-L at ply 2 across fields, in place of the -101..55 spread.
(c) Yes, the capped LR control takes forget 10 too, so the only factor between it and T-L-Ne-grow is the rule;
    and the league's LR lines restart on forget 10 (main.rs's own default). For the record: forget applies to
    the CNBP routers as well (their counts divided at a split), which is the "copies that forget" design and
    is what the probit lines have been running at forget 1 without, so if the league's probit trees ever move
    to forget 10 that is a second factor to test, not to assume.

6.63 (06:55, 11-12). diversity-p2c (v331; +-28): cfp300 minus bfp300 73 (my "within 30": wrong in this arena),
cfp331 minus bfp450 12 (inside), cfp300 minus pfp300 51, cfp300 minus dfp300 -1 (6.57 said 20-80: wrong),
cfp331 minus dfp314 37, tree minus flat -18 / -10 / 8 (inside), ladder against pfp300 9 / 77 / 37 at v100 /
v150 / v200. The window cut the cluster references at cycle 10-11 and the F-P references at cycle 19 (F-P
lines) and 97 (one tree line); cold, the cut cluster references beat the lines' versions of that cycle by
162 and the F-P references by 37 (and still 23 at the end).
(a) Reading: the 73 at v300 is not established. The triangle at v300 (cfp - dfp -1, dfp - bfp 6, cfp - bfp 73)
    is off by 66, the ladder is non-monotone (9, 77, 37), and the ceiling read is 12. Taken together, p2c =
    diversity-p2 = bfp within the noise of these arenas: at ply 2 the ceiling is the same in all four regimes
    (pure-4, pure-6, weaker foreign references, references at the lines' level under the window). A kick-start
    from 18 cycles of strong early games is possible and unresolved; it is not worth another run, because
    the window itself was cutting the wrong games (b), so the regime as run was not the one designed.
(b) The window's rating is unsound, and this is the evidence: in-run ratings from T 0.1 play misorder MODEL
    KINDS, not just versions. The cluster-tree references rated ~150 below the F-P references in-run and beat
    the lines by 162 cold at the cut, where the F-P references were 37 over: the calibration-slope bias of
    the log-odds softmax at T 0.1 (about 132 Elo per ln slope; 6.28 side note: trees 1-6 at T 0.1 against 118
    cold) is kind-specific, under-rating trees by 100-150. Every in-run decision runs on it: the window, the
    crownings, patience and retirement, and the global league's STANDINGS, which the user reads as the ranking
    (the first cluster tree retired first). Fixes, cheapest sound first: (1) rate on a separate cold set, T
    0.02, each cycle, at a quarter of the training volume (about 100 games an entry at 8 entries, SE ~35), used
    only for ratings, the window, crownings and patience, with the training games unchanged at T 0.1 and the
    margin widened to 50 to match the SE; no pooling across versions (the user's rule); cost +25% games. (2)
    One policy for both, no extra games: an exploration rule that does not scale with the model's confidence
    (epsilon-greedy over the model's ranking, or a softmax over ranks), so that every kind follows its ranking
    with the same exploration and the T 0.1 training-data properties are kept; elegant, but it changes the
    training stream and needs one ply-1 field read cold before adoption (policy as the one factor). (3) Per-
    model calibration correction: already in place and insufficient (cal-own does not remove the bias at ply
    2). Recommend (1) now and (2) as the test to run before the next league if the user prefers one game set.
    Also for the user: re-read the first global league's standings in the light of a ~100-150 under-rating of
    trees in-run; the cold reads (error trees 98-118 over F-P at ply 1) are the truth of record.
(c) No change to the ply verdict from p2c; wait for fponly-p2. What stands: at ply 2 no regime beats a pure
    field at equal versions, the window is needed but must run on sound ratings, and the ply-2 ceiling of a
    field is set by its top's own play.

ranking is biased against partitioned models by about 100, and the user's first league ordered its field
wrong. No new read is needed for the error-tree figure: diversity-p2's frozen references are error-routing-
p1's v314 pair, whose cold read exists (error-routing-p1-cold: T-P-Ne 77 +-22 over F-P at ply 2, 118 at ply 1)
against in-run ~15 as references at ply 2; so error trees are under-rated by ~60 in-run at ply 2 and cluster
trees by ~110, the same order. Both figures go with recommendation (1).
6.63 note 3 (07:30, 11-12). Fresh read of the same v314 pair at ply 2 (errref-cold-p2, 600 games): T-P-Ne 43
+-28 over F-P, against the older 77 +-22 from error-routing-p1-cold (a different field and arena); in-run as

quoted: a frozen pair's cold contrast moves by 30 between arenas.

6.64 (07:50, 11-12). grow5's offline smoke (P1, forget 10) reverses 6.62 (a), and I agree. In each pair the
first trials share the leaf and children, so the shadows alone make the difference: the forget-10 copy
without a split beats the unforgotten leaf by ~0.004 nats per example over a 1M trial; under the "none"
shadow the third trial is kept on the boost alone. So the as-built shadow is "boost without split", the fair
alternative when a boost is available, and it isolates the partition; "none" credits the boost to the split, a
bias FOR splits, and its kept count would run high whatever splitting is worth. My premise that the
alternative to a split is "the leaf as it would have continued" was wrong because the boost is an action in
its own right. The LR trials' 40-60% failure as built is then the partition's real value at these leaves
UNDER THIS LEARNER: children that cannot adapt do not specialise, and the one-window trial sees that truly.
(a) grow-l-p1c is not worth running: "none" is biased by construction, and its Elo read would say only whether
    boost-credited splits help in play, which is not the rule question and not a league setting. Keep the
    flag at "split" (as built); the slot goes elsewhere.
(b) Yes, the finding underneath is learner-level: AdaGrad's accumulating sums make an LR leaf's steps decay as
    1 / sqrt(life), too slow for a stream that moves (the league) and, by the offline smoke, even for a leaf
    that has not converged on a stationary pool (a one-pass leaf is still learning when its steps have
    already died: the snapshot gains were positive all run). A periodic or split-time forget is a crude
    version of a decaying second-moment estimate; the principled version is RMSProp-like sums with a decay
    set so the effective memory is about one to three windows (beta with 1 / (1 - beta) of 1-3M examples),
    which keeps steps alive under drift and converges to AdaGrad's behaviour on a stationary stream over that
    memory. Test it on the FLAT first, one factor at ply 1: F-L AdaGrad x2 against F-L decaying x2 (and F-P x2
    as the usual anchor if the no-extra-lines rule allows), floor 300, read cold at plies 1-2 at v150 and the
    final. Prediction: decaying F-L over AdaGrad F-L 10-40 at ply 1 and 0-30 at ply 2 (faster tracking under
    drift; the flat's late improvements are today throttled by its dead steps), and within 0.002 nats on a
    stationary held-out slice. If it holds, the LR family's trees are re-asked on top of the better learner
    (children then adapt without a boost, and the forget flags return to 1 for leaves), and the LR seat's
    fragility at ply 2 (6.59 c) is likely the same defect. A build, as you say; it precedes any further LR tree
    run. The probit learner needs nothing: ADF's tau^2 dynamics are already the decaying memory.
(c) Agreed: forget 1 had the best held-out loss offline (0.6469 against 0.6525 and 0.6570), so boosting at
    splits buys prequential speed at a cost in precision on a stationary stream, and grow-l-p1b's forget 10 (-13
    at ply 1, 75 at ply 2 against forget 1) is that trade in a drifting field. A decay with a long memory is
    the version of the boost that keeps the speed without the precision cost; that is what (b) tests.
6.64 note (08:05, 11-12). lrdecay1 design checked: S <- S (1 - 1/N)^Delta + g^2 per coordinate, lazily by stamps,
is the right form and better than an RMSProp mean. With one-hot features a coordinate is touched at its
feature's frequency f, so at steady state S ~ f N E[g^2 | touched] and the step is AdaGrad's at life N for THAT
coordinate: rare features keep their larger steps as in AdaGrad, the rate needs no retuning, and for n << N
it is AdaGrad exactly. An RMSProp mean would lose the per-coordinate frequency scaling and force a retune.
Delta must be the node's examples since the coordinate's last touch (the node's clock, not the stream's),
which for a leaf on a third of the stream makes its memory a third as long in stream terms, which is right:
memory in own examples. On N: 2M is the step of a 2M-life model, about 6x AdaGrad's step at the flat's
80M life, which may be hot late in a run; add N 8M to the offline sweep (1, 2, 4, 8M), and pick the LARGEST N
whose prequential gain on the drifting stream is within 10% of the best, since the stationary held-out loss
is the precision cost and grows as N shrinks. Prediction for the sweep: prequential gain over AdaGrad 0.003-
0.008 nats at N 1-2M falling to 0.001-0.003 at 8M; held-out cost on P1 0.000-0.003 at 8M, 0.003-0.008 at 1M;
N 4M the likely pick. For the LR tree leaves on this learner, --forget-factor returns to 1 (the decay is the
boost, applied always), and the probit lines are untouched. lr-decay-p1 as drafted is the right first field.

a little dynamics predicted slightly better than none" on a STATIONARY one-pass pool, i.e. the same situation

flag (--probit-tau, kept in the model file). The probit is the stronger family and the ply-2 flat is the best
evaluator we have, so this is the lever on the goal itself, with no build.

6.65 correction (08:50, 11-12). Pick 1 is withdrawn: its premise was false on the record. probit-settings (11-09,
a probit-only ply-2 league stream, 500 cycles) had tau 1e-4 below the default 1e-5 in every window (-26 to
-61), tau 0 and 1e-6 level, and the drift study (11-10) shows why: a probit weight's variance sits at the tau
equilibrium from v25, holding its last ~145k uses, about one cycle's batch, and no stream outdates a frozen
model at any lag up to 100 cycles. So the probit already forgets on a one-cycle memory, its step floors rather
than decays, the AdaGrad analogy fails, and ten times tau cuts the memory below one batch. The probit's
dynamics are tuned and closed; --probit-alpha is untested in a league but predicted nothing offline (per-game
powers no better than 1) and is not worth the slot. I should have checked RESULTS before proposing a run that
the record had already answered.
The slot goes to diversity-p2d, with its power fixed in the READ, not the run: a 600-game cell is +-28 at 95%
(SE 14), so a 30-Elo contrast needs about four times the games, 2,400 per contrast per version (SE 7, +-14).
Do it by restricting the read arena to the contrast's own entries (d and c lines at equal versions, 8
entries, no bfp/pfp) at 300 round-robins instead of 75, at v300 and the final separately (no pooling across
versions), ~1-2 CPU-h of reads; the run's replicates (2) and cap (400) need no change, since the pooled cell
already combines both replicates of each arm. Prediction unchanged: d over c 0-40 at ply 2 at equal versions
(centre 15), d over bfp450 at the final 0-40, ply 1 within 20; rule: 30 or more over c at both reads says the
window as rated cost the ceiling; within 15 at both says it cost nothing here, which is also informative
given that the cut references were stronger cold (then the lines did not need those games, and the window's
bias is a standings problem, not a strength one).

6.66 (09:10, 11-12). fponly-p2 (F-P x4, 1,584 games; x4 cells 1,200 games, +-20; v300 / v320): x4 minus bfp 65 /
57 at ply 2 and 12 / 52 at ply 1; x4 minus pfp 78 / 28 at ply 2 and 39 / 70 at ply 1; bfp minus pfp -53 / -10
at ply 2 and 83 / 27 at ply 1. Against 6.61: the ply-2 composition effect holds at the top of its band; the
ply-1 coupling fails (x4 is level or above bfp at ply 1, so the weaker siblings' games gave nothing there
either); volume does not harm, and the 1,584 standard stands.
(1) The account, with the coupling dropped: the value of a training game at ply 2 is set by the QUALITY OF PLAY
    AT THE POSITIONS THE TRAINEE REACHES. Games of its own kind at its level or above are useful and their
    volume helps at both plies (x4 over pfp: 2.2x the useful games, 28-78 at ply 2, 39-70 at ply 1); games of
    weaker players in its own distribution (own past, weaker sibling kinds on the same stream) corrupt the
    labels and cost at ply 2 (bfp: ~60 against x4) while adding nothing at ply 1; games of weaker FOREIGN
    players label positions it does not reach and are neutral (diversity-p2). The coordinator's useful-games
    count is this account made countable: bfp's useful volume ~5/9 of 1,584 (its own kind's games) ~ pfp's
    720, and bfp = pfp at ply 2. What it forbids: an F-P x4 field at 720 games beating x4 at 1,584 at either
    ply (volume of useful games must help; prediction the reverse by 20-60); a mixed field whose window runs
    on COLD ratings failing to recover x4's level (prediction bfp-with-cold-window = x4 within 20 at ply 2);
    and weaker foreign play harming at ply 2 anywhere. A result against any of these ends the account.
(2) The next league at ply 2. If the six train together, F-P's batch carries the trees' games (10-50 weaker at
    ply 2, the LR trees more), which costs it ~60 at ply 2 unless the window excludes them, and the in-run
    window is biased against trees by 30-110: for F-P that bias happens to exclude the right games; for the
    flats it also excludes level trees' games that are useful (the cluster tree rated 110 low while level),
    and for the trees it makes them train on the flats' games, which is harmless. So the window must run on
    sound ratings (6.63's recommendation (1)), margin 50, and the league should expect its top model to train
    on its own kind's games and its equals': the pure regime, which x4 says is the best one. The design
    consequence: the standard of 1,584 games a cycle counts ALL games, but a line's USEFUL volume is its own
    pairings plus its equals', which shrinks as the field diversifies; the self-games mechanism already
    exists, so top up each line's useful volume to the standard with self-games (own current against own
    champion), scaled per line from its window's kept share. That is the one league rule this series adds.
(3) p2d's read and predictions stand. Note the consistency: on the bfp zero at v300, x4 65 and p2c's F-P 73
    (its window cut the references early, so its lines trained mostly on their own games): "no weaker play in
    the batch" is worth about 60-70 at ply 2 whichever way it is achieved, and diversity-p2's 6 is the weaker
    foreign play being neutral rather than useful.
Order: lr-decay-p1 next when the build reports; nothing before it. For the user, the cleanest statement of the
ply-2 regime from this series: the best ply-2 evaluator so far comes from a field of its own kind at full
volume, and every admixture of weaker play in its own distribution costs it.
6.66 amendment (09:25, 11-12). The ply-1 clause of (1) is wrong as grouped: own past (replay) HELPS ply 1 (38 /
37) while costing ply 2 (-123 / -64), and weaker sibling kinds (bfp against x4) are level to harmful at ply 1
(-12 / -52) while costing ply 2 (-65 / -57). Restated: at ply 2 both cost 60-120 and the clause stands; at
ply 1 the two differ, and the difference is what the games are. Own past is the lineage's own play when it
was weaker: the SAME positions with the blunder structure of shallower play, which by 6.51 is a ply-1-like
value function, useful at ply 1 and wrong at ply 2. Weaker siblings are a different KIND at the same time on
the same stream: positions the trainee reaches, labelled by play that is worse without being shallower (a
tree's errors are partition errors, not search-depth errors), so there is nothing in them for ply 1 either.
What the restated clause forbids: own-past replay failing to help at ply 1 in a ply-2 field (it must, by
20-60), and weaker-sibling games helping at ply 1 anywhere (they must not, within 20). Drop the earlier
"nothing at ply 1" for own past; keep it for siblings.

6.67 (09:45, 11-12). lrdecay sweep: every memory N loses on every stream (drift -0.0007 to -0.0041 calibrated,
abrupt change -0.0018 to -0.0111, stationary held-out cost 0.0015-0.0063), halving per doubling of N, losses
growing along the drift stream; lr-settings-2 (on-policy) had the SMALLER late rate win. 6.64 (b) is false:
the LR flat's late steps are not too slow, if anything too large, and lr-decay-p1 stays unlaunched. The three
facts fit together: a boost helps in-window tracking prequentially (grow5's smoke: +0.004 for a boosted copy
over 1M) but costs precision (forget 1 best held-out offline), so applied permanently (decay) the precision
cost dominates and applied once at a split it buys fresh children their divergence from the parent. That is
the fork's reading, and it is right: the split boost is a transient need of fresh children, not a learner
defect.
(1) LR's grow-trial failures, now: with the shadow boosted alike (as built), the trial isolates the partition
    and LR's 40-60% undone trials are the partition's real value at LR leaves under this learner, about half
    the probit's (kept gains 0.002 against 0.011-0.014), consistent with the LR family's ply-1 gains being
    half the probit's all along. The LR seat stands as 6.62 had it: T-L-Ne-grow at forget 10 (6.59's rule
    passed), with the stated caveat that its trials fail often and its value is the smaller; if the user wants
    the LR side simple, F-L plus the capped LR tree at forget 10 is the fallback, which (i) below would
    qualify.

METHOD. Within-run tree-against-flat cells are not strength verdicts: d's tree beats d's flat by 61 direct but
by 22 or -37 routed through strangers, c's by -42 direct against -48..11 routed, at 4-6 SE. Two things are in a
within-run pair: CO-ADAPTATION (the two lines trained for 300 cycles on games against each other, and a
partitioned model can specialise against a sibling's style more than a flat can, so the inflation is
asymmetric) and kind-against-kind MATCHUP (style). Same-kind cross-run cells, which carry every one-factor
verdict so far, have neither; the coordinator's check says they are unaffected, and that is what the theory
expects.
(1) Yes: architecture verdicts come from a common-opponent read. For each contrasted entry, score it against a
    PANEL of strangers (lines of both kinds from other runs, same version and ply, never a line it trained
    against); the verdict is the difference of the two entries' panel results (a BT restricted to the panel and
    the two entries, or the mean score against the panel); the direct cell is reported as the matchup, not the
    verdict. At risk, in order of weight: (a) the ply-1 tree gain itself, ~100 over F-P in every field, which
    was within-field everywhere (G2, error-dag-probit, error-growth, grow-p1/b/c); the offline-pairs 77 had no
    co-adaptation (neither model played) but still the matchup; (b) "in a pure ply-2 field the flat beats both
    trees" (base-probit-p2, -40..-12) and the ply-2 ties (probit-late -3, confirm, trees-p2), which were within-
    field and can move either way; (c) the global champions' cold read (one co-adapted league). The first
    panel read to run, minutes: ply-1 trees against ply-1 flats of DIFFERENT fields at equal versions (G2's
    trees against error-dag-probit-p1's flats and the reverse; grow-p1's against grow-p1b's), same recipes,
    no shared games. Prediction: the cross-field tree-over-flat at ply 1 is 40-80, reduced from ~100 but
    positive, and the ply-2 panel read of base/late/p2c/p2d trees against other runs' flats moves the ply-2
    trees to within 20 of the flats, up from -10..-40. If the ply-1 panel gain falls under 30, the six's tree
    seats rest on co-adaptation and the architecture story is rewritten; I do not expect that, since the
    offline pair, free of co-adaptation, gave 77.
(2) The six and the ladder: the composition does not change until the panel reads are in, but the user should
    hear now that the magnitude of the ply-1 tree gain and the ply-2 ordering are to be re-read, and that the
    thunderdome's standings are by design a co-adapted field: right as the ranking the user asked for (it is
    the game), matchup-laden as an architecture verdict.
(3) capped-l-f10-p1: both arms are the same kind, so the matchup term is small, but co-adaptation applies to

6.73 (13:50, 11-12). Spec for pick 3, uncertainty-weighted training (the one objective lever that passes the
constraints: it uses nothing but the model's own output).
1. The weight. s = the model's expected score for white from its own three-outcome prediction, s = P(W) + P(D)
   / 2; raw weight u = 4 s (1 - s): 1 at s 0.5, 0 at a decided position. It targets the white-against-black
   axis that move ranking uses; a sure draw gets weight 1, which is acceptable because the games are 90%
   decisive, and a draw-aware variant (three-outcome entropy over ln 3) is the second variant if ever wanted,
   not the first. Floor: u is clipped to [0.2, 1] BEFORE normalisation, because a model that is confidently
   wrong would otherwise never see the example that corrects it (weight ~0 at s near 0 or 1); the floor keeps
   every example training at a fifth of full weight at least.
2. Online: the pre-update prediction of the model being trained, which both learners already compute before
   their step; no snapshot.
3. Normalisation: w = u / m, where m is a running mean of u over the node's own examples with memory N =
   1,000,000 (m <- m + (u - m) / min(n, N), n the node's example count; m starts at the first u), stored in the
   state; cap w at 3. So the mean effective weight is 1 and the learning rate is not changed on average,
   which is the confound to avoid; the weighting only moves effort from decided to undecided positions.
4. Learners. F-P (ADF): the likelihood power. --probit-alpha already raises each position's likelihood to a
   power; apply the per-example power alpha_x = alpha * w through that code path (the fractional moment-
   matching update: the v and w shifts scaled by alpha_x), so the mechanism is the existing one and alpha 1
   with w 1 is today's bytes. F-L (AdaGrad): the update uses w g; the accumulator takes the UNWEIGHTED g^2, so
   the weight changes the size of the step and not its normalisation (if both scaled by w AdaGrad would
   normalise a steady weight away). Trees: leaves take the weight by the same paths; routers do not (their
   target is which child predicts best, unweighted); the grow trial's statistic stays unweighted log-loss.
   The run is flats only, so the tree rules are for the record.
5. Flags: --weight-by-uncertainty {none | score} default none (bytes unchanged), --weight-floor 0.2,
   --weight-memory 1000000, --weight-cap 3. Training options, kept in the state, not the model file (play is
   unaffected and hybrid17 plays the models as they are).
6. Run (weight-p2): pure ply-2 field, F-P x2 unweighted and F-P x2 weighted (score), seed 58, games_per_cycle
   1,584, self-games on, floor 300, cap 400, hybrid17 games; the weighted lines train on the new binary and the
   unweighted on it too with the flag off (byte-identical to hybrid6, so one binary). Panel read against y +
   v + l at v299 plus x4 and x4b as strangers (both strangers to seed 58), at v150, v300 and the final, plies 2
   and 1. Side read, offline and cheap: both finals' full log-loss and winner log-loss on a P2 held-out slice,
   overall and restricted to undecided positions (|s - 0.5| under 0.3 by the unweighted model); the weighted
   model should be worse in full loss by 0.005-0.02 (it de-emphasises decided positions) and better on the
   undecided subset, which checks that the weighting does what it says before the Elo is read.
Prediction and rule as given: weighted over unweighted 0-40 at ply 2 against the panel, within 20 at ply 1;
30 or more adopts it for the league's flats; within 15 closes the objective as a lever. One more branch: 30 or
more BELOW says the decided positions carry information the ranking needs after all (the endgame calibration
is not inert), which would also close the lever and would say something about the loss that becomes Elo.
