6.76 (15:00, 11-12). ply24-panel, ply-2 half (panel, +-20): f3 minus x4 -186 / -245 (ply 3: 147 / 182), x4
minus bfp 45 / 57 (ply 3: -2 / -7), w4 minus f3 61 (ply 3: -81), pfp minus bfp -5, rtr minus x4 -481 (ply 3:
-83). Pre-registered ply-4 predictions, before that half lands.
Decomposition used (6.20-6.22, 6.31 b): a model trained at ply t read at ply r scores S_t(r) = A_t - D |r - t| +
P [r = t mod 2]: a base A_t, a depth cost D per ply of distance, a parity bonus P at the training parity.
From the flats at plies 2-3 with a common D: f3 - x4 gives (A_f - A_x) + P + D = 165 and (A_f - A_x) - P - D =
-215, so P + D = 190 and A_f - A_x = -25 (the swing of ~380 is 2(P + D), not 2P); w4 - f3 (-81 at ply 3, 61 at
ply 2) then gives P - D = -48, so D ~ 120, P ~ 70, A_w - A_f ~ 110. The ply-1 tree does not fit the linear form
(its -481 / -83 needs a larger even-ply penalty), so its prediction is empirical.
Predictions at ply 4 (panel, +-20): f3 - x4 and f3b - x4b: f3 LEADS, 0 to 130, centre 60 (depth beats parity
when the read is two plies from x4 and one from f3; the 11-11 "flat-p3 over flat-p2 by 100 at ply 4" is the
same contrast with a weaker ply-2 flat). w4 - f3: 150 to 300, centre 220 (w4 at its own ply and parity; the
11-11 173 was an earlier version in another arena). x4 - bfp: 0 to 40, centre 15 (the pure-field lead is ply-
2 precision, same parity at ply 4 but two plies away). rtr - x4: -550 to -350, centre -450 (even ply, three
plies from its training). pfp - bfp: -20 to 30, centre 5. Replicates within 15.
What would break the decomposition: x4 over f3 at ply 4 (parity beating depth at distance two: D under 50);
w4 - f3 under 100 (the warm ply-4 rung carrying less than its own ply should give); x4 - bfp over 60 (the
pure-field lead being general rather than ply-local, against the ply-3 read).

6.77 (15:20, 11-12). The user's cohort question (how much plateaued same-architecture models deviate within a
setup against across setups), on the library fork's design: F-P only, plateau-ended, ply-2 lines at ply 2 and
ply-1 lines at ply 1, final and final-20, ci15, BT over unrelated cohorts, nested components with measurement
error subtracted. Pre-registered SDs (Elo, against strangers, at T 0.02), ply 2 / ply 1:
  version within line (final against final-20 at plateau): 10-15 / 10-15 (in-run version SD 17 carries a
  share of matchup; cold against strangers less);
  replicate line within run: 5-10 / 5-10 (observed 0 to -4 at plies 2-3);
  run within setup (seed replicates): 20-30 / 25-40 (x4 against x4b -1, weight-p2's controls 37; the ply-1
  fields showed a +-50 seed term for trees, and flats are steadier);
  setup: 30-45 / 20-35, with the caveat that at ply 2 the setup term is not a random effect but a known
  systematic one (pure against mixed composition: 60-95; own-past replay -117; frozen references ~0), so
  the component should be reported with the pure/mixed split shown, not only as an SD; at ply 1 the fields
  were all of one kind (flats with trees) and the term is smaller;
  matchup (within-run direct cell against the BT prediction): SD 25-35 at both plies (observed 25-49 off).
  Measurement at ci15: +-15 per entry, SE ~7.5, so components under ~10 are at the edge of resolution and need
  the version pair pooled per line.
Design points to fix before launch: (1) training length is confounded with setup (floors 300-600 and plateau
stops judged at T 0.1), so read every line at a COMMON version (v299) beside its final, and attribute final-
minus-v299 to length, not setup; (2) games_per_cycle (720 against 1,584) and ply1_entries (the old probit-
settings fields) are setup covariates to record and, if lines with both exist, to split on; (3) lineage: p2c
and p2d are identical through v9 and share references (one run, or drop one); warm-started lines are not
plateaued from scratch (exclude or flag); the weighted lines of weight-p2 are a different learner (exclude,
keep its controls); (4) binaries: flats trained on hybrid6 and the grow/weight binaries with flags off are
byte-identical, so no binary confound, but record the binary per line in the index; (5) the version level
should be measured as each line's pair difference against the panel, not as two BT entries that are near-
copies; (6) "unrelated cohorts only" must keep games between different-seed runs of ONE setup (they are
unrelated lineages; that is the run-within-setup term) and drop only same-run and shared-lineage pairs; (7)
the global league's F-P (Elo window, co-adapted thunderdome, killed at cycle 4079) is its own setup and its
"plateau" is a different rule; include it flagged, not pooled. Prediction for the user's question in one
line: within a setup, same-architecture plateaued flats differ by 20-30 at ply 2 (25-40 at ply 1) run to
run and under 10 line to line; across setups the spread is 30-45 at ply 2, most of it the pure/mixed
composition, and 20-35 at ply 1; the matchup term is as large as the run term, which is why the library's
arenas must read against strangers.

6.78 (15:40, 11-12). treeonly-p1 (the user's uniform-field test with observers): T-P-Ne capped x4 play at ply 1;
F-P x2 observe (train on the shared set, never play); comparator probit-f10-p1's capped forget-1 trees and F-
P from a mixed field; panel r + e + g + h at v300, v150 and the final, plies 1 and 2. Pre-registered (Elo
against the panel, +-20; ply 1 / ply 2):
(a) uniform tree minus mixed capped tree: 20 to 70 (centre 40) / 0 to 40. The pure regime for the stronger
    kind: a mixed field's trees train a third of their batch on the flats' play, 85 weaker at ply 1, which by
    6.66 costs at the training ply and little off-ply (fponly's 95 at ply 2 and 12-52 at ply 1 are the flat's
    version of the same thing).
(b) F-P observer minus mixed F-P participant: -20 to 30 (centre 5) / -30 to 20. The observer trains entirely
    off-policy on a stronger kind's play at its level, which 6.66 rates useful-to-neutral, and loses only its
    own games (a third of a participant's batch) and the co-adaptation that the panel does not pay for
    anyway. The new question this cell answers: whether a learner that never plays keeps up with one that
    does, i.e. whether the self-play loop's on-policy positions matter for a flat at ply 1; a deficit of 30
    or more says they do.
(c) F-P observer minus uniform tree, identical data: the tree ahead by 60 to 120 / 20 to 60: the architecture
    gain with the data source held fixed, free of the flats' play in the batch for either; this is the
    cleanest architecture cell yet, and the number to quote for "what the partition is worth at ply 1".
Design: run fponly-p1 (F-P x4 playing, T-P-Ne capped x2 observing, ~1.5 CPU-h) in PARALLEL, not after: the 2 x 2
(learner x data source, each kind both playing and observing) is interpretable only with both fields, the
panel read is the same, and sequencing saves nothing but time. Its cells: uniform F-P minus mixed F-P 0 to
50 / -20 to 30; tree observer minus mixed tree participant -40 to 20 / -30 to 20 (a tree learning off-policy
on the weaker kind's play in its own distribution, which 6.66 says costs at the training ply); tree observer
minus uniform F-P on identical flat data: tree ahead 30 to 90 / 10 to 50 (less than (c): the flats' games
lack the positions where the partition pays). Two things to fix in the spec: observers get NO self-games
(participants' self-games are ~9% of their batch; record the difference rather than top up, since a top-up
would make the observer play); and observers are versioned on the same cycles as participants so the panel
reads align. Ply 2: repeat only if (a) is 30 or more at ply 1 and (b) is within 30; then the ply-2 version
asks whether the uniform tree field escapes the ply-2 trained-tree deficit (-25 against strangers), which is
the one way left for trees at ply 2, at ~4 CPU-h.

6.79 (16:00, 11-12). For the record: w4 minus x4 at ply 2 is -131 against the panel (the linear fit of 6.76
gave -155: inside its crudeness); the fit's ply-1 values, written before the ply-1 arena lands, are f3 - x4
-75 and w4 - x4 -155, rtr outside the form.
Architecture-by-parity sweep (the user: do architecture advantages follow parity?). Measure tree minus flat
within a field, against strangers, at playing plies 1-4, for ply-1-trained pairs and ply-2-trained pairs.
Entries (28): ply-1-trained, r, e, g, h at v300 (F-P x2 and T-P-Ne x2 each; h's tree is the bar-on grow1,
labelled); ply-2-trained, y, v, l at v299 (F-P x2 and T-P-Ne x2 each). b's cluster tree and DAG are second
kinds and go in a later sweep if this one shows a parity effect. Panel structure: one arena per playing ply,
all 28 entries, one BT over all games with same-run pairs' games dropped; each pair's verdict is its two
entries' BT difference, so every entry is scored on 24 strangers of both kinds and both training plies.
Rounds: 20 round-robins at plies 1-3 (+-28 per pair, +-14 pooled over the four ply-1 fields); at ply 4, 10
round-robins if cores are short (+-40 per pair, +-20 pooled), 20 if affordable; the pooled ply-1-field mean is
the test statistic.
Predictions, pooled tree minus flat (panel):
  ply-1-trained pairs: r 1: 60-110 (both hypotheses; known ~85); r 2: 25-65 (both; known ~44); r 3: PARITY
  says 50-90 (back to the training parity), DISTANCE says 0-30 (two plies away); r 4: parity 25-55, distance
  -20 to 15. My own point prediction is a mix, a parity-following component on a decay: r 3 ~40 (30-60), r 4
  ~15 (0-30).
  ply-2-trained pairs: r 2: -40 to -10 (both; known -25); r 1: parity -10 to 20 (the deficit is an even-ply
  thing), distance -15 to 5; r 3: parity 0 to 20, distance -15 to 5; r 4: parity -35 to -10, distance -10 to
  10. These cells separate the hypotheses by ~20 only; the ply-1 pairs at r 3 and r 4 are the decisive ones.
What separates them: the ply-1 pairs' r 3 against r 2. Parity predicts r 3 at or above r 2 (the gain comes
BACK at the training parity); distance predicts r 3 below r 2 by 15 or more and r 4 near zero. A mix shows
as r 3 between r 2 and the r 1 value with r 4 positive but small. If parity holds, the user's conjecture
("trees better at ply 3 again, worse at ply 4") is the ply-1-trained family's prediction, and for a ply-3
rung the architecture choice would follow the rung's parity: trees for odd rungs, flats for even; if
distance holds, architecture gains are local to the training ply and the rung's model is chosen at the rung.
Order: plies 1-3 at once, ply 4 after the current ply-4 arena frees its cores, as planned.
6.79 addendum (16:15, 11-12). The ply-1 read kills the linear symmetric fit wherever x4 plays: f3 - x4 46-63 at
ply 1 (fit -75), w4 - x4 -5 (fit -155), while w4 - f3 holds (-66 against -80) and reads -66 / 61 / -81 across
plies 1-3, a clean parity alternation for a same-lineage pair with little distance cost. An asymmetric fit
(playing SHALLOWER than the training ply costing ~150 a ply, deeper ~50, P ~70) repairs the ply-2 and ply-3
cells but still misses f3 - x4 at ply 1 by ~100: no additive depth-and-parity model fits across these
lineages, and the pure-field x4's value function is ply-2-specialised in a way a ply-3 LR flat is not. So
6.76's decomposition is retired as a predictor across lineages; two things survive it: parity alternation is
real in same-lineage adjacent-ply pairs (w4 / f3), and the model trained at a ply wins at that ply at every
ply so far (rtr at 1, x4 at 2, f3 at 3). Fold into the sweep: (1) the hypothesis test stays on the ply-1-
TRAINED pairs' r 3 against r 2 (one family, one training ply; parity against distance within it), which
needs no cross-lineage fit, predictions as filed; (2) add the same-lineage adjacent-ply pairs as parity
probes, w4 / f3 now and x4 / the ply-3 rung's F-P when it exists, read at plies 1-4: parity predicts the sign
alternating with the playing ply and a magnitude of 60-80 at every ply, distance predicts the magnitude
growing with the distance between the plies; (3) the asymmetry is the replacement hypothesis for the cross-
lineage cells: a model read shallower than it trained loses 100-150 a ply, read deeper 20-50, to be tested on
the rung's ply-3 F-P at plies 1, 2 and 4 against x4 (prediction: the ply-3 F-P under x4 at ply 1 by 50-150,
over it at ply 4 by 50-150, and the ply-2 cell parity-dominated, x4 ahead by 100-200).

6.80 (16:40, 11-12). Encouraged router splits in a growing DAG (the user reopens router splits: copies add
parameters that may pay over a long horizon and may enable better leaf splits later; router-p1 could not see
that, its leaves being capped and finished before most router splits, and HG's immediate-gain test rejects a
delayed payoff by construction). Agreed that 6.54's closure covered capped DAGs and immediate gains only.
1. Rule, "encourage then prune": router splits come on a STEADY ALLOWANCE (one per D_r root positions, D_r
   10M = one per ~50 ply-1 cycles, so a leaf-trial window of 25-30 versions follows each), made whether or not
   a benefit shows; the allowance decides the rate, the evidence decides only WHERE: the eligible router
   (age three own windows, children ready by the 90% share guard, not a copy) with the highest G_real -
   G_null, ties to the highest H_v, then the lowest index; the shadow partition as 6.36 (path where the router
   has several arrival paths, else the cluster shadow). No bar at the split. Pruning is a LONG do-no-harm
   trial: after the split the shadow pair keeps running as the unsplit alternative for K = 5 own windows, and
   the split is undone only if its paired statistic is BELOW -2 block-SE at the end of K windows (clearly
   behind after many windows); otherwise it stays whatever its gain. Chains: a copy cannot router-split
   (--router-chain 1), which keeps the path-context cells at most 3x the nodes; without it a chain of k
   router splits multiplies the cells by 3^k (the 11-10 bddctx worry), and a linear-form context would be
   the alternative if chains are ever wanted. Leaves in both arms: grow4's rule with the bar ON.
2. Arms, one factor (the allowance on against off): F-P x2 (anchor); T-P-Ne-grow bar-on x2; D-P-Ne-ctx-grow
   with the allowance x2, whose DAG with the allowance off is the tree exactly (identity to be checked). Ply
   1, new seed, 1,584 games a cycle, floor 600, cap 900 (long: ~20 leaf trials and ~12-18 router splits by
   v600-v900), hybrid17 play. Log per router split: version, router, arrival paths, the chosen-by statistic,
   the long-trial statistic per window and the verdict at K; per leaf trial: version, leaf, whether it lies
   under a router split made earlier, kept or undone, gain; the kept rate under router splits against
   elsewhere (the user's mechanism); path cells' mean |cell| and their movement per window; node and cell
   counts, the 55-node warning.
3. Read against the ply-1 panel (r + e + g + h at v300) at v150, v300, v600 and the final, plies 1 and 2; DAG
   minus tree is the router splits' net value with everything else equal; the structural read is the kept-
   rate table (leaf trials under router splits against elsewhere) and the fraction of router splits pruned.
4. Predictions: router splits 12-18 by the final, of which 30-60% pruned at K; DAG minus tree against
   strangers -15 to 10 at ply 1 and -25 to 15 at ply 2 at v300, -20 to 20 / -25 to 15 at v600 and the final
   (centres -5); leaf trials under router splits kept at the same rate as elsewhere within 15 points; cells
   moving by under 0.05 nats. What confirms the user's hypothesis: a kept rate under router splits 20 or more
   points above elsewhere AND the DAG 20 or more over the tree at the final at both plies; that would be the
   first measured long-horizon value of routers and would reopen 6.54. What closes it: the DAG within 15 of
   the tree with the kept rate level, after 600-900 cycles with router splits forced on schedule.
5. Build (grow6): grow4's leaf rule inside bdd (grow4 refuses path context and router rules), grow3's
   shadows and stats, path context, plus --router-split-rule encourage, --router-credit D_r, --router-trial-
   windows K, --router-trial-z, --router-chain, --log-router-stats; byte-identical with the flags absent; the
   identity test that a DAG with the allowance off trains byte-identical to the grow4 tree; unit tests for
   the allowance, the priority order, the long trial's K-window verdict and undo (shared children restored,
   copies removed), the chain cap and the cell count. One binary can carry all of it; the leaf-rule-in-bdd
   merge is the main work. Queue behind treeonly-p1 and fponly-p1 unless the user wants it sooner.

6.81 (17:10, 11-12). Cohort read (RESULTS section 1, 11-12 ~10:50; 85 entries and 478k games at ply 2, 72 and
307k at ply 1; BT over unrelated runs). SDs, ply 2 / ply 1 (my bands in brackets): version 11 / 8 [10-15],
mostly runs' lines drifting together; replicate line 7 / 0 [5-10]; seed at equal length 0 / 21 [20-30 /
25-40]; between set-ups at equal length 48 / 9 [30-45 / 20-35], 18 / 16 at the finals; matchup 0 within a
run, 13 between unrelated F-Ps [25-35]: the 40-70 matchups seen before were KIND pairs (tree against flat),
not co-training. Effects at ply 2: replay -106 at v299 and level by v542 (slower learning, same plateau);
frozen references 10-27 better; 720 games -22 at equal version, level at the finals; PURE MINUS MIXED 15 +-1
against an all-F-P field (24 against 3 at equal length, 37 against 17 at the finals), where the 43-95 came
from panels with trees and F-L.
(1) What it does to the composition account and the headline. The within-kind strength effect of
    composition is 15-20, not 60-95: training with weaker sibling kinds' games costs a flat about 15 against
    other flats, and the remaining 40-80 of the "pure-field lead" was how the two flats fare against TREES
    and F-L, a kind-matchup term, not general strength. So 6.66's clause stands at a fifth of its size, and
    6.71's revised account is the record: at ply 2, own-kind games at one's level are worth ~15-20 over a
    mixed batch within kind, own-past replay slows learning without lowering the plateau, foreign references
    are neutral to slightly useful (10-27), and volume beyond ~700 useful games adds ~20 at equal versions
    and nothing at the plateau. Corrected headline for the user: "the pure F-P field's F-P is 15 stronger
    than mixed-field F-Ps against other F-Ps at equal length and ~20 at the finals; its 43-95 leads were
    against tree and F-L opponents, a matchup effect; against the ply-1 cohort the global league's F-P is
    level." Why a flat that never saw a tree beats trees better than one that trained with them is open; the
    measurement structure is not: kind-pair cells carry 40-70 of matchup, same-kind cells carry 0-13.
(2) Panel rule for treeonly-p1 / fponly-p1: report every cell against THREE panels (F-P-only, tree-only, the
    standing mixed), and the verdict depends on the contrast's kind. Same-kind contrasts ((a) uniform tree
    against mixed tree, (b) observer F-P against participant F-P): the SAME-KIND panel is the verdict
    (matchup-free), the other panels report the matchup. Cross-kind contrasts ((c) observer F-P against the
    uniform tree, and every tree-against-flat cell): there is no single verdict, since kind matchups of 40-70
    are real play; report the F-P-panel and tree-panel numbers as the two faces and the mixed panel as the
    thunderdome's view, which is the practical one for the user's league. 6.78's bands were written against
    the standing mixed panel and stand there; against the same-kind panels, (a) and (b) should read 0-30 and
    -15..15 (the within-kind composition effect is ~15-20, as the cohort read says), and (c) remains the
    architecture number, 60-120 at ply 1 against the mixed panel, lower against F-Ps and higher against trees.
(3) Anchor: pin the mean of the STANDING PANEL's entries at zero (the panel is fixed across arenas, so every
    arena lands on one scale; a single pinned entry would add its own +-8-15 to everything, and a field mean
    shifts with the field). Where no standing panel plays, the field mean, and no random at all.

6.82 (17:30, 11-12). The in-run rating decision (the user: add T 0.02 games; train on them if it does not hurt,
else rate on them only). Design for the twins of the uniform ply-1 runs.
1. Share and arms. Keep S = 0.25 (396 cold games a cycle at 1,584; at 8 entries ~100 games an entry, SE ~35,
   which is why 6.63 set the window's margin at 50; the rating precision is a league choice, not this test's).
   The volume confound of "add 25%" is ~8 at equal versions and 0 at the plateau (6.71), small, and the
   better fix than a replace arm is to make the test's comparison between arms that play the SAME games:
   twin A, cold_games {0.02, 0.25, train true}; twin B, cold_games {0.02, 0.25, train false}, the user's
   fallback regime (rate on them, train without them). A minus B is the training effect of the cold games at
   equal volume played and equal in-run decisions; B minus control (the running runs) is the effect of
   changing the in-run ratings alone on a run's dynamics. Three arms per uniform field (control running, A
   and B as twins); a replace arm is not needed.
2. The rule, operational: "does not affect strength negatively" = A minus B, against the SAME-KIND panel (F-P-
   only for the F-P field, tree-only for the tree field; 6.81's verdict rule), at or above -15 at the training
   ply (ply 1) at BOTH v150 and the final, with the interval's lower bound above -30; ply 2 reported, within 30
   expected either way; the mixed panel reported as the thunderdome's view. If met, the cold games join
   training in the leagues; if A minus B is below -15 at either read, the fallback (B) is the rule.
3. Predictions (same-kind panel, +-20): A minus B at ply 1: F-P field -15 to 5 (centre -5), tree field -25 to
   0 (centre -10): a 25% admixture of near-argmax play (narrow, the data that cost 106 at ply 1 when it was
   all of the training) costs a little, and trees overfit narrow data more. B minus control: within 15 at
   equal versions at both plies (the training data are identical; only crownings and the stop change). My
   expectation is that the rule is met for the flats and borderline for the trees; the user's plan then has
   the cold games training the flats' leagues and the trees read with care, or the fallback for all if the
   tree cell fails, since one rule for all lines is simpler than two.
4. Pairing. The plateau stop runs on the in-run ratings, which differ between the twins by construction, so
   the finals would land at different cycles and the stop itself would be a confound: run the twins at a
   FIXED length (min_cycles = cap = 300), read at v150 and v300, and read the control at v300 if it ran that
   far, else at its final and the twins' same version. Seeds as the controls' (59, 60): the twins share cycle
   1 and part when the cold games enter, which is what "twin" should mean here. One more thing to log: the
   crowning count and the window's cut cycles in A and B against the control, which is the direct measure
   of how much the in-run decisions change when the ratings become cold.

6.83 (17:50, 11-12). Parity sweep, plies 1-3 (tree minus flat against 24 strangers): ply-1-trained family 89 /
36 / -27 at plies 1 / 2 / 3; ply-2-trained family -9 / -34 / -17. My "~40 at ply 3" and the parity hypothesis
both fail: the tree's lead shrinks MONOTONICALLY with distance from the training ply and goes negative two
plies away, in both families (the ply-2 family's lead is negative at its own ply and least negative one ply
either side, which is the same shape around a negative value). Distance, not parity, is what architecture
advantages follow, through ply 3; 6.31 b's distance reading was right and its parity addendum was not, for
trees. Parity remains real for same-lineage flats (w4 / f3), i.e. parity is a property of the value function
a flat learns at a ply, and the tree's extra is a local correction on top of it that decays with distance.
(1) Ply 4, pre-registered: ply-1-trained family -70 to -30 (centre -50; the decay continuing, three plies
    away); ply-2-trained family -30 to 0 (centre -15; two plies from its ply, as its ply-1 cell). A ply-1-

    evaluators with a lead that is gone by ply 3, so the league's purpose is the ranking and the ply-1

6.84 (18:10, 11-12). (A) Ply 4 of the 26-line read: f3 minus x4 104 / 87 (my 0-130, centre 60: inside, high);
w4 minus f3 144 (my 150-300: just under); x4 minus bfp -16 / -11 (my 0-40: under; the pure-field lead is

-193; f3 63 / -186 / 147 / 104; w4 -5 / -131 / 62 / ~248), with the adjacent ply at the other parity the worst
cell for every model: so parity lives in a model's value function and is strongest one ply away, while the
tree-minus-flat lead within a family decays monotonically with distance (6.83). Both true, consistent.
(B) flatply-p1 (ply-3 and ply-4 flats against ply-1 flats at ply 1): the model trained at a ply wins at that
    ply, every ply so far, so the ply-1 F-L over f3 / f3b by 80-180 (centre 130), over w4 by 150-250, and the
    ply-1 F-P (probit-f10-p1's) over x4 by 150-220. The user's second question has that answer in advance; the
    read confirms or bounds it.

    question and would confound the architecture cell), MIXED field (T-P-Ne capped limit 4, 1M, noise 0.03 x2
    and F-P x2 at entry ply 3) so it is comparable with the ply-1 and ply-2 families' cells, and 720 games a
    cycle rather than 1,584 (volume is worth ~20 at equal versions and nothing at the plateau; this halves a
    ply-3 run, ~3-4 CPU-h at the coordinator's prices), floor 300, cap 400, hybrid17 games, hybrid6 training.
    Read against a ply-3 panel of strangers (f3, f3b, w4, x4, x4b, bfp: all flats, since no ply-3 trees
    exist, so the tree-minus-flat cell is "against flats" and said so) at v150 and the final, plies 2, 3 and 4;
    the within-field cell as the matchup. Prediction: tree minus flat at ply 3, trained at ply 3, -40 to 10

    30-90. Rule: 30 or more at the final keeps the odd-ply hypothesis alive and sends it to ply 4 (a ply-4
    mixed field, same form); 0 or less says the tree's advantage is a ply-1 phenomenon (the shallow-play value
    function of 6.30) and parity has nothing to do with architecture, which closes the user's conjecture.
(C) The user's end rule (no retirement; the league stops on the experiments' plateau rule, crownings on cold
    ratings; the field constant) is better than 6.83's, which still had retirement in it, and I withdraw mine.
    One parameter to set, not a flaw: chance crownings. At 2 SE on ~100 cold games a line a cycle (SE ~35), a
    line crowns by chance about 2.3% of cycles, six lines ~14% of cycles, so a short window would almost
    never be crowning-free; the experiments' scaled window (K 35-50) has stopped every experiment and is the
    one to use, with the crowning bar at 2.5 SE on the cold set and the cap as the backstop. With that, the
    rule stands as the user's.

6.85 (18:40, 11-12). probit-p3 launched (seed 61; measured 80-100 CPU-s a cycle, so ~8-10 CPU-h to the floor,
not my 3-4: my ply-3 price was wrong by 2-3x, noted). flatply-p1 landed before my prediction: ply-1 F-L over
f3 / f3b 142-177 (inside 80-180), over w4 205-220 (inside), ply-1 F-P over x4 225 (just past 220); the model
trained at a ply wins at that ply, now at every ply 1-4.
The user's new direction: (1) a GLOBAL LADDER, an occasional arena with no training; (2) the old global league
as an ELO-FILTERED multi-kind EXPERIMENT for insight, not hard conclusions.
(a) The ladder. Anchor: no random; pin at zero the mean of a FIXED reference set, the standing panels (r + e +
    g + h at ply 1, y + v + l at ply 2, f3 / f3b / w4 at plies 3-4), so the ladder's scale persists across its
    occasional runs and matches the experiments' reads. Players are (model, depth): each library final at
    its specified ply, one entry per model, 8-16 entries per ply from the plateaued library (the best lines
    of each kind per ply); a few anchors (the panel F-Ps) may enter at a second ply, their ply +-1, only to
    calibrate the depth axis, labelled as such. Rounds: cost is the ply-3 / ply-4 games; at 40 entries and 10
    double round-robins (~780 games an entry, SE ~12, +-25 on a contrast) the ply-4 side is ~0.8 CPU-h and the
    whole ~1.5-2 CPU-h, run after each batch of library additions. What a mixed-ply fit says: the ranking of
    DEPLOYED players (evaluator plus its search), which is what the user asked for; what it cannot say: the
    strength of an evaluator apart from its depth, or a transitive scale across plies, since a model swaps by
    ~380 between plies and kind pairs carry 40-70 of matchup. Report the BT with its residual (matchup) SD and
    the largest pair deviations as part of the result, not as noise; within one ply the ladder is a ranking,
    across plies it is a map with known folds.
(b) The filtered experiment. Rule, as read: a game enters line L's set iff every player in it is rated at or
    above R_L minus m on the COLD ratings of the cycle; m = 50 (about 1.5 SE of a ~100-game cold set); the
    top line then trains on its own kind's games and its near-equals', few, and is topped up with self-games
    to the standard (6.66's design consequence; the top-up is new code: self-games scaled per line by its
    kept share). Ply 1 (the lineup's evidence is there and games are cheap); lineup the six of update 8; end
    rule the user's (no retirement, plateau stop, crownings at 2.5 SE on the cold set, scaled window, cap).
    What we know: the reference window showed nothing detectable in p2c / p2d on biased ratings, and weaker-
    kind games cost ~15-20 within kind (6.81), so the filter's effect on the TOP line should be small; the
    insight is in the rest of the field: the flats train mostly on the trees' games (a stronger kind at ply
    1), the trees on tree-only games (treeonly-p1's regime), and the field's data flows upward only. That
    dynamics (do weaker kinds catch up faster from their betters' games; does the ranking stay stable; what
    co-adaptation does under a filter) is what no one-factor run shows. Predictions against an unfiltered
    mixed twin, against the standing panel at ply 1: flats 10-30 better, trees 0-40 better (6.78 (a)'s uniform-
    tree effect), ranking unchanged (trees over flats by 60-110 against the mixed panel, 40-70 of it matchup);
    at ply 2 everything within 30. Yes, it waits for the cold-games twins: the filter on T 0.1 ratings is the
    biased one we have already run, and on cold ratings it is a different experiment.
(c) Build list: the ladder needs arena.py's per-entry ply, the pinned reference mean, and the residual
    report; the experiment needs the Elo filter generalising the reference window, cold_games from the
    observers fork, the per-line self-games top-up with the kept share logged, and the end rule with the
    cold crowning; the library index needs each model's specified ply. The rating-history cap matters less
    with no open-ended league, agreed, but the experiment still runs to a plateau and should keep per-cycle
    history only inside the scaled window. Nothing else missing.

6.86 (19:10, 11-12). Uniform-field read (same-kind verdict panels; v299, ply 1 / ply 2): (a) uniform tree minus
mixed tree -14 / -4 (v150 -29 / -73); (d) uniform F-P minus mixed F-P -29 / -58; (b) F-P observer on tree games
minus mixed F-P 0 / -64; (e) tree observer on flat games minus mixed tree -144 / -151; cross-kind on identical
data, mixed panel: (c) F-P observer minus playing tree -54 / -107 (tree ahead); (f) tree observer minus playing
F-P -32 / -34 (FLAT ahead). Four of my six bands failed, (f) with the sign reversed. No ply-2 uniform follow-up
(6.78's rule, the coordinator's decision): agreed.
(1) Account. Two terms set a learner's strength here: ON-POLICY data (its own games, where its own value
    function is tested and corrected; worth ~45 at ply 1 by the 2 x 2, more at ply 2) and the QUALITY of the
    other players' games relative to its level (a stronger kind's games help, a weaker kind's cost). At ply 1
    trees are the stronger kind. The mixed field gives the flats the trees' games (worth ~30 to a flat: (d) is
    what the flats lose without them) and gives the trees the flats' games (worth ~0 to a tree at ply 1: (a)
    is what the trees gain by excluding them, nothing). That is also why the pure F-P field led by 15-20 at
    ply 2 and trails by 29 at ply 1: at ply 2 the other kind is WEAKER (trees trained at ply 2 sit 34 below
    flats), so excluding its games helps the flat; at ply 1 the other kind is STRONGER, so excluding them
    hurts. One rule covers both: games from at or above your level help, from below cost, and a pure field
    removes both kinds of game at once. The observers add the on-policy term: the F-P watching stronger
    players loses on-policy and gains all-stronger data and nets 0; the tree watching weaker players loses
    both and nets -144, the largest cell, because a tree's leaves specialise to the positions its own play
    reaches and a weaker kind's play does not reach them. (c) and (f) say the same: on the trees' games the
    playing tree beats the watching flat by 54 (architecture plus on-policy, less the flat's gain from
    stronger data); on the flats' games the playing flat beats the watching tree by 32 (on-policy plus the
    tree's loss from weaker data, less architecture).
(2) The 2 x 2 is not a clean decomposition, because the two data sources differ in quality by a term as
    large as the architecture estimate: taking the quality terms at their measured sizes (~30 gained by a
    flat on tree data, ~25 lost by a tree on flat data), (c) gives A + O ~ 84 and (f) gives O - A ~ 7, so
    architecture ~40 and on-policy ~45 at ply 1; the uncorrected 11 / 43 is a lower bound on architecture
    and an upper bound on on-policy. At ply 2 the quality signs flip and the same correction gives
    architecture ~20-40 and on-policy ~60-80, roughly. The firm statements: on-policy data is worth as much
    as the architecture, and observers are a poor way to train a model, so every line should play.
(3) The Elo-filtered experiment is this result's natural home, and my 6.85 predictions change: in a six-line
    field at ply 1 the filter removes only the flats' games from the trees' sets (worth ~0 to them, (a)) and
    changes nothing for the flats (they already train on the trees' games, which the filter keeps), so
    against an unfiltered mixed twin: trees -15 to 15, flats -10 to 10, ranking unchanged. The one place a
    gain could come from is the top-up: self-games add ON-POLICY data to the top line (worth ~45 per the 2 x
    2, with the volume-of-useful-games term ~20), so a top line topped up to the standard may gain 10-30. The
    experiment's value is the dynamics and the mechanics of a cold filter, as the user said: insight, not a
    strength gain; the strength result is already here: train every line on its own games plus its betters'.
(4) Cold-games rule: unchanged, with the note that the on-policy term comes from the positions a model's own
    T 0.1 play reaches and T 0.02 games reach fewer of them, so the admixture's cost should be larger for
    trees, as 6.82 has it. probit-p3: unchanged (-40 to 10 for tree minus flat at ply 3); the sign of mixing
    effects at ply 3 will follow which kind is stronger there, which that run also tells.

6.87 (19:30, 11-12). The filter's rating. The fork is right: a floor at the current version's cold rating on
72-98 games (ci95 +-110-125) moves by the margin every cycle and the cut follows noise. Ruling, two parts.
(i) A line's OWN games always count (filter on the opponents only): the on-policy term is worth ~45 (6.86) and
a filter must never remove it; the filter then decides only which games between OTHER players enter, kept
iff both players' ratings are at or above R_L - m. (ii) Smooth every rating used by the filter: R_L and the
other entries' ratings are each the running mean of that entry's last k = 5 versions' cold ratings (SE ~25 a
rating, ~35 on a difference; champions and references accumulate across cycles anyway); margin m = 50 on
the smoothed ratings (about 1.4 SE of the difference). Not the champion's rating as R_L: it is steady but
lags the line (older, weaker, and it jumps at crownings), which would set the floor too low and keep weaker
games; not a margin scaled to the SE: at SE 55 it would be ~85 and the filter would rarely cut, i.e. the
experiment would not run its own factor. Log the kept share per line per cycle; expected in the six: the
flats' games cut from the probit trees' sets (85 below), the LR tree borderline, the flats keeping everything
(nothing is below them), and the top-up filling the probit trees to the standard with self-games.
(B) Agree: run both, the twin is what every prediction is relative to and without it the run is dynamics
only; the pair is 3-7 CPU-h, and the twin's filter-free top-up-free field is also the control the cold-games
twins will want.

these sizes; chain 3 would be 27 and starve the cells), so the possible splits are ~2x the routers, 10-14 by
v600; D_r 5M with the bank at 2 makes credits effectively always available, so the rate is set by

6.91 (21:30, 11-12). t002 redo: all T 0.02 minus all T 0.1 at v299 (same-kind panel): F-P -101 / -138 at plies
1 / 2, trees -63 / -54 (my bands: trees at the edge, F-P missed, worse); the fifth dose c - b: F-P -32 / 49,
trees 26 / -12. The user: one game set for training and fair ratings, with randomised openings (hybrid19,
--opening-plies K). Drafts: the t002 runs plus openings, comparators coldrate and t002.
(a) K = 6 plies, UNIFORM random (not model softmax), one factor. What T 0.1 supplies and T 0.02 lacks is
    coverage, divergence of games into positions the argmax lines never reach; six uniform plies (three
    moves each) make every game start from a different, often unbalanced position, which is coverage of the
    kind a search queries (6.45), and the rest of the game is then near-argmax play, which is the data the
    user wants to rate on. A model softmax at high T keeps the model's own opening style and diverges less;
    uniform is the stronger dose and the cleaner factor. If K = 6 closes most of the gap, K is then a knob (4
    against 8) and not a design question.
(b) Yes, the opening plies are training positions: they are the coverage, their labels are the game's
    outcome like every early position's, and a search will be asked about positions like them; excluding
    them would keep the games' divergence and throw away the positions it was bought for.
(c) Pre-registered, same-kind panel, v150 / v299, plies 1 / 2. Open minus coldrate (T 0.1): trees -90 to -30 /
    -40 to 0 at ply 1 and -90 to -30 / -50 to 0 at ply 2 (centres -20 and -25 at v299); F-P -150 to -60 / -70 to
    -20 at ply 1 and -180 to -80 / -90 to -30 at ply 2 (centres -45 and -60): openings recover about half of the
    T 0.02 gap at the plateau. Open minus t002: trees 20 to 60 / 20 to 50 at ply 1, 20 to 60 / 20 to 60 at ply 2;
    F-P 50 to 120 / 40 to 80 at ply 1, 60 to 150 / 50 to 100 at ply 2. Two fields are two replicates of the
    openings effect; one run per arm resolves ~90, so the F-P cells can be read and the tree cells only
    bound.
(d) Agree, within ~30 of T 0.1 at the plateau and the rating-only set goes, with one caveat that is a check,
    not a bias: random openings add outcome variance from unbalanced starts and compress the Elo scale
    (gaps between models shrink by perhaps 10-20% at K = 6, uniformly across kinds), unlike T 0.1's softmax
    bias, which is kind-specific because it scales with each model's confidence. A uniform compression leaves
    rankings, windows and crownings right and only shortens the gaps; measure it as the ratio of the in-run
    fit's gaps to the cold panel's, and if it is under 0.8 widen the margins by the inverse rather than
    keep a second game set.

6.92 (21:45, 11-12). Sub-cycles (the user: train every cycle, rate and crown every Nth). The coordinator's
build is right (cold games only on rated cycles; floor and cap in training cycles; the plateau window in
rated cycles; the filter on the last rated version's R_L and SE).
(a) N = 4, as proposed, for these reasons: the per-test gain is ~4 cycles of learning against the same SE
    (~23), so early tests gain power and late ones are noise at any N; champions and filter floors at most 4
    cycles stale, which at ~1-5 Elo a cycle is inside the SE; crowning counts fall by ~4x, so the plateau
    window must be set in rated cycles with the same number of TESTS as before (the experiments' scaled K
    counted tests, so K rated cycles = 4K training cycles; do not shrink it); version noise is unchanged in
    kind (a candidate every 4th version samples the same +-17 SD, and a champion crowned on a noise high
    stays 4 cycles longer, which is cheap). N 8 would make champions and floors ~8 cycles stale, past the
    early run's per-cycle gains, and halve the plateau resolution; N 2 buys little.
(b) Pitfalls: champion entries change less often, so the field's games against the champion are from a
    staler model, a small loss of on-policy freshness (the champion is the self-games' opponent too), which
    at N 4 is a few Elo and should be logged as the champion's age; the top-up's "champion above the floor"
    test uses stale ratings between ratings, fine; and the cold set at share 1.0 every 4th cycle doubles that
    cycle's cost, so wall time per rated cycle doubles while the mean stays at 1.25x.
(c) Not moot. If K = 6 openings let one game set train and rate, the rating is free every cycle, but the
    champion TEST still needs the current-against-champion comparison, and crowning every cycle at 2 SE on
    one cycle's games is the noise crowning of 6.63; rating every N cycles on N cycles' worth of one-set games
    (pooled over the N versions of the same window, which the user accepted as sub-cycles) is then the
    better test for free. The sub-cycle idea survives the openings result as the crowning schedule.
(d) Adopt it as a mechanics change, not a test: its effect on strength is predicted under 20 (stale
    champions and floors), which one run per arm cannot resolve (~90), and its purpose is cost and decision
    quality, which the logs measure directly (cold-game share, crownings per rated cycle, champion age). Pre-
    registered for the record if it is ever paired: rate-every-4 minus every-cycle, same seed and rating set,
    against the panel: within 20 at both plies at v300, crowning counts within 30% of a quarter of the every-
    cycle run's, plateau reached within 50 training cycles of each other.

6.93 (22:30, 11-12). (a) Non-transitivity across training plies: at ply 2, P2 - P1 324, P3 - P1 314, yet P2 - P3
196 directly (chi2/dof 1.98, excess SD 51), and P3 matches P2 against the P1 panel. Two things: a panel 300
below the contrasted entries is SATURATED (both score ~95% against it and the scale cannot separate them),
and between near-equals the parity edge shows directly (the P2 model plays ply 2 at its own parity, the P3
model at the wrong one, and P2 exploits P3's parity-specific errors the way neither can exploit the far
weaker P1's any further). Consequences: a panel must sit within ~150 of the entries it judges (the standing
panels are fine within a family; for cross-ply contrasts build the panel from both families' flats); and
"strength" is one number only within a family and ply; across training plies it is a directed graph with
parity edges, and the ladder's mixed fit must report its excess SD as part of the result, not absorb it.
(b) Ply-4 field: start_forget 1 for F-P (the probit's variances sit at the tau equilibrium with a one-cycle
    memory, so a warm F-P adapts in a few cycles; W4's forget 8 was LR's AdaGrad sums); trees as built. The
    ply-1-trained DAGs start ~300 below the P3-started lines at ply 4; their games are weaker play in the
    warm lines' distribution for the first 50-100 cycles (a dilution of ~15-20, 6.81) while they train on
    stronger games and should catch up at the field's rate; accept it as the price of the user's "DAGs that
    may split", flag it in the read, and if the DAGs are still 150 behind at the floor, say they never did.
    720 games, the cycle numbers and the plateau are fine. Pre-registered (panel of ply-4 strangers, +-25):
    P4 F-P minus its P3 start at ply 4 100-200 (centre 150) by the floor, at ply 3 -60 to 0, at ply 2 -150 to
    -50 (parity); P4 minus P2 (x4) at ply 4 150-250; trees minus F-P at ply 4 -30 to 20 (the user's odd/even
    idea says -40 to 0); DAG minus tree -30 to 20; the DAG routers split 2-6 more times by the floor (chain
    2 allows it), 30-60% undone.
(c) Classmates: in-run ratings order classmates at chance (55%; only gaps over 2 SE agree, 75%, and those
    are 16% of pairs). Change: no line within a run is ever ranked by its in-run rating; in-run ratings serve
    decisions only (crownings, floors, the stop); every ordering claim comes from an arena or panel read, and
    in-run gaps are quoted with their SE or not at all.
(d) Draw-aware SE (real noise 0.60 of nominal): adopt it in new experiments, and with rate_every 4 crown at
    3 draw-aware SEs, which is ~1.8 nominal, about today's false-crowning rate, honest about the draws;
    2 draw-aware SEs would be ~1.2 nominal and multiply noise crownings. Cold share: 0.5 at ply 1 (cheap;
    draw-aware SE ~19-22 at share 0.5 on a rated cycle) and 0.5 at ply 4 as drafted (0.3 CPU-h a cycle is
    affordable; 0.25 would push the SE past 30 and the floors into noise).

6.94 (23:10, 11-12). Results: probit-p3 at ply 4 tree minus F-P 1 (17 / -1 / 1 at plies 2-4: trees tie flats
when trained at ply 3; my 10-50 at ply 3 missed, the odd-ply idea is dead); the trainply matrix at ply 1 P2 -
P1 -282, P3 - P1 -176, P3 - P2 76 (non-transitive); openings, F-P field: T 0.02 with 6 random plies minus T 0.1
0 / -12 at v299 (level; my -45 / -60 missed, better), minus plain T 0.02 105 / 136; nocells: path cells 8 / -5
in play against 0.002-0.013 nats offline.
a. Plateau window under rate_every 4: the window that matters is in CYCLES, not tests. A test compares the
   current to a champion fixed since the last crowning, so the last test in a window of L cycles sees the
   whole gain rL; at r ~1 Elo a cycle and a bar of ~40 (3 draw-aware SE) any window over ~40 cycles catches
   a still-improving line, and extra tests inside it add little. So my "4x" (K tests = 4K cycles) was wrong,
   it doubles run length for nothing; the coordinator's 2x (K/2 tests = 2K cycles) is the closer and is what
   to launch with, plus one guard against a single noise-low final test: do not stop if the last test's
   current-minus-champion is above 1 draw-aware SE (a slope check). Crown at 3 draw-aware SEs if the build
   is verified by launch, else 2 nominal (about the same bar, 6.93); record which. Changing the window mid-
   run before any stop decision (before cycle 300 with floor 300) is safe for repeatability: the window is
   a stopping time, and no game or training step depends on it.
b. DAG values: the user's "too liberally" is the credit's doing (5M, always banked) more than the chain's;
   chain 1 bounds splits by the routers and ends "encouraged" once each has split, which drops the long-
   horizon case the user reopened. Pick credit 10M, chain 2, router-trial-z -2 (a kept split must beat the
   unsplit by 2 block-SE; 14 of grow-dag-p1's 16 kept would pass, so it prunes the two null ones and keeps
   the rest). Prediction: nodes 35-45 at v300 and 55-70 at v600 (grow-dag-p1 had 63-76 at v420 on 5M / chain
   2), i.e. ~40% fewer as told, with the trees at 19-25. With chain 1 instead: 30-40 and 45-60.
c. Two separate runs per standard recipe at ply 1: yes. It is cheap, the run-to-run SD (35-45) is the main
   unknown and the thing that sets every later read's resolution, and the library gets two plateaued models
   per recipe, which the ladder and the panels need anyway.
d. For the user: (1) trained at ply 3, trees tie flats at every ply, as at ply 2; the tree's gain is a ply-1
   phenomenon (the shallow-play value function), so ply 1 is the screening ground for architecture and the
   deeper evaluator is, for now, the flat trained at its ply; (2) the ply-1 matrix is non-transitive by
   design of the models: a ply-3 flat is less wrong at ply 1 than a ply-2 one (same parity) yet both lose to
   the ply-1 model by 180-280; strength across training plies is a directed graph (6.93), and the ladder
   should show it as such; (3) openings work: six random plies make near-argmax games as good as T 0.1 for
   training a flat and level by v299, so one game set can train and rate, pending the tree field (the user's
   worry that early-game randomness biases against trees is exactly what that read answers; pre-registered
   -40..0 for trees minus T 0.1); (4) the path-context cells add nothing in play (the fourth case of offline
   log-loss not becoming Elo), so the standard DAG can drop --path-context's cells and keep the unsharing by
   path, which is simpler and faster at ply 4 (DAG games cost 8 CPU-s there).

6.95 (23:40, 11-12). Results. grow-dag-p1 final (v599, panel): DAG minus tree -20 / -8 (v300: -38 / -21), DAG
minus F-P -11 / -3, tree minus F-P 9 / 5: liberal router splits (5M, chain 2, 63-76 nodes) gained nothing and
cost 20-40 early; by 6.80's rule the long-horizon hypothesis is not confirmed, and the standard's tighter
values (10M, chain 2, trial-z -2) are the right correction, not a vindication. Filtered minus twin: no
detectable gain (-20 / -2, signs flipping), as predicted under one-run resolution; its dynamics are the
result. Openings, tree field: T 0.02 with openings minus T 0.1 3 at ply 1 and -109 at ply 2, and -48 against
plain T 0.02 at ply 2: my -50..0 missed badly. Reading: at the training ply the trees are level, so the
openings are not biased against trees' learning; what they remove is T 0.1's exploration along the WHOLE
game, which is the coverage of the positions a 2-ply search queries, and a tree's leaves, specialising to
position types, lose that coverage where a flat generalises over it (the flats were level at ply 2). So one
game set is fine for flats and costs trees ~100 at off-ply reads; since the standard's models are read at
ply 2 and feed the ladder, the rule stays T 0.1 training games plus the rating-only cold set, and openings
are closed as the single-set route for mixed fields. The user's worry was right in effect, wrong in place.
The ply-2 rung. Recommendation: WARM as the standard (s1 -> p2-s1, s2 -> p2-s2, replicates independent),
plus ONE scratch run (seed s1's recipes, a third seed) as the calibration arm, once, to settle warm-against-
scratch for every later rung; three runs, ~30 CPU-h at ply 2, against the ply-2 standing panel at equal
ply-2 cycles (v75, v150, the plateau). Evidence: the ply-3 rung equalled from-scratch at a fifth of the CPU,
W4 gained 173 over its start, and the reheating cost (27-51) was LR's. Prediction: warm equals scratch within
25 at the plateau and reaches scratch's v150 level by v50-75, which is the saving; rule: scratch 30 or more
over warm at the plateau says the warm start's specialisation persists and rungs start cold, otherwise warm
is the rule for every rung. Settings: games 1,584 at ply 2 (the standard); plateau min 41 / cap 101 rated
cycles under rate_every 4 (164-404 training cycles, enough: a warm rung's plateau comes early); start_forget
1 for probit (the tau equilibrium adapts in cycles), 10 one-off for LR flats and LR tree leaves (the ply-2
value function is ~300 from the ply-1 one and dead AdaGrad steps would take hundreds of cycles to move; W4
used 8 and gained 173), the LR routers' counts divided with it, acceptable. Grow lines keep their structure
and grow state (the rung continues growth; the credits accrue from the ply-2 stream anyway), with their
windows, shadows and snapshots reset at the start (fresh statistics on the new stream, 0.3 of the spec).
DAGs: warm-start sensibly if any router trial in flight is CLOSED at the start (kept on its statistic so
far, or kept outright; no trial straddles the plies), the shadows and null shadows start fresh, and the
path tables carry over (inert in play, 6.94, so harmless; the standard may drop them anyway).

A. Why growth does not pay, ranked: (1) CAPACITY ON THESE FEATURES is not the bottleneck: every model is a
   linear table over one-hot square-piece indicators partitioned by phase-like routers; the partition's
   worth saturates at ~9 leaves (the third level copies, 6.28-6.32; grow's extra leaves buy nothing), and
   nothing in any model represents an interaction between two squares; (2) data per leaf at deeper plies
   (the flat is data-hungry there and leaves starve, which is the lag of 6.44-6.46); (3) coverage, worth ~45
   on-policy and lost by trees first when exploration drops; (4) the split criterion (error = cluster,
   routing headroom ~0, so not the lever); (5) the field (D). Experiments, in order of what could break the
   ceiling, each one factor against the standing panel at ply 1 unless said:

6.97 (00:50, 11-13). Launches noted: cvf-p1 (24 pairs) and cvf-p2 (12), explore-t015-p1 (std s1 at T 0.15, same
seed), probit-field-p1 (the four probit recipes x2 = 8 lines, matching the std field's size, games and data

S7, the smallest build (for the user's ruling; the proposal is 6.26 c-4 made concrete). Start from the flats'
training binary (the probit leaf in errdag4-src probit.rs, ADF over one-hot indicators); F-P only, no trees:
a feature-growing probit. A pair feature is the conjunction of two active indicator values (feature i at
value a AND feature j at value b), a binary input like any other, with a Gaussian weight. Candidates: every
pair of the 70 features' active values, ~400k pair-values in all, 2,415 active per position. Statistic: the
first-order score at the current model, which for ADF is free: after each example the standardized shift v
of the latent is known, so each active candidate accumulates S += v and n += 1 (two floats per candidate,
~3 MB a model); z = S / sqrt(n). Every 1M positions add the top K = 50 candidates with |z| above 5 (about the
Bonferroni bar for 400k candidates, sqrt(2 ln 400k) ~ 5.1) as features with mu 0 and s2 = (prior / 2)^2
(shrunk), cap the total at 2,000 pairs (a 3x model at most), and drop a pair whose |mu| is under 0.5 sqrt(s2)
after 3M of its own examples, returning it to the candidate pool. Zero knowledge throughout: no pair is
named, the data choose. Cost: the candidate accumulation is ~35x the 70-feature update per position; if too
slow, accumulate for a fixed hash-chosen quarter of the pairs (deterministic, knowledge-free), 4x the cost
of the plain F-P in training, no change in play beyond the added features' lookups. Flags: --pairs on/off
(bytes unchanged when off), --pairs-k 50, --pairs-z 5, --pairs-cap 2000, --pairs-shrink 0.5, --pairs-drop
0.5; the pair list saved in the model file so hybrid plays it (the fixed-point path needs the pair lookups
or falls back to float). Power: within one field, F-P plain x2 against F-P S7 x2, the field effect cancels
and the panel read's noise is ~23 on the difference (run term 18, panel SE 14), so 2 seeds read 50 and more
cleanly and 30 marginally; the expected size if interactions matter is 50-120 (one partition bought 85), so
2 seeds suffice, and a result of 15-40 gets a third seed before judgement. Read at plies 1 and 2 against the
panel (ply 2 is where flats lead and capacity may matter more), v150 and v300.

6.98 (01:15, 11-13). Residual leaves approved by the user; spec (resid-probit/summary.txt read).
1. Design check. hybrid6rp's design IS the trunk-plus-residual I asked for, in a hierarchical form that is
   better than a single root trunk: every delegator's shadow trains on every example through it (the root's
   on all), a leaf predicts shadow + delta, and the trunk therefore tracks the moving ply-2 stream at the
   flat's speed whatever the leaf's share, which is the lag mechanism of 6.44-6.46 addressed where it bites.
   Shrinkage: with --residual-inherit-sums the delta starts as the copy's variances, so the delta moves as a
   plain leaf would (no shrinkage), but the tracking comes from the shadow, not from a slow delta, so inherit-
   sums is the right first form and needs NO new build; a shrunk delta (a smaller delta prior, e.g. 0.1
   against 0.3, a one-flag build) is the follow-up variant if the trunk alone does nothing, not the first
   arm. Without inherit-sums the delta's prior variances change the predictions' spread from the split on
   (the summed model's variances are the delta's), which is a second effect; do not use it. Flags for the
   arm: hybrid6rp with the capped T-P-Ne's exact flags plus --leaf-residual --residual-inherit-sums; nothing
   else changes; flats and the plain trees on hybrid6 (hybrid6rp with the flags off is hybrid6).
2. Experiment, residual-p2: pure ply 2, FROM SCRATCH (the lag is a ply-2-training phenomenon; a warm start
   from ply 1 would confound it with the rung), F-P x2, T-P-Ne capped x2 (limit 4, 1M, noise 0.03, forget 1),
   T-P-Ne capped + residual x2; one seed with replicates (ply-2 run SD ~5, field effect shared within the
   field; a second seed only if the result lands 15-40); 1,584 games, self-games, rating-only cold set as the
   standard, floor 300, cap 400; ~5-7 CPU-h (the shadows add a little training). Read against y + v + l @299
   at v150, v300 and the final, plies 2 and 1; within-field cells as matchups. Bands (panel, ply 2): residual
   minus capped 20-60 (centre 35: the lag removed); residual minus F-P -10 to 40 (centre 15); at ply 1 residual
   minus capped -20 to 20. Rules: residual 30 or more over capped at ply 2 says the trunk beats the lag and
   trees carry past ply 1 (then the ply-3 twin and the shrunk variant follow); within 15 says the lag is not
   the trees' ply-2 problem after all and the partition itself has nothing to add at ply 2. No ply-1 twin:
   the ply-1 tree already wins and the residual's job is ply 2; the standard can carry a residual recipe later.
3. The T3 LR residual run (mixed ply-1/2 regime, "no lasting gain") is not evidence against this: at ply 1
   there is nothing for a trunk to fix (the plain tree wins and the field plateaus), T3's regime was not the
   one where leaves lag, and its LR deltas had the dead-step defect (6.59). Probit at ply 2 differs on both
   counts: the lag is the ply-2 problem, and ADF deltas move by their variances. Honest expectation: a gain of
   20-60 over the capped tree, bringing the tree level with or a little above the flat at ply 2, not a model
   that "clearly outperforms" it; that bar still needs the features (S7, parked).
6.98 addendum (01:25, 11-13). S7 approved too; the experiment spec, on 6.97's build.
(a) Flat replicates in one field train the identical model (deterministic training on shared data, as the
    fponly-p2 lines showed within 1 Elo), and S7 is deterministic as well (the hash subset and the selection
    are fixed), so within-field replicates are one model twice. The replication is two SEEDS as separate
    runs: s7-p1-s1 and s7-p1-s2, each a two-line field, F-P x1 plain and F-P-S7 x1, ply 1, 1,584 games (R 132
    over 4 entries), self-games, rating-only cold set, floor 300, cap 400. A two-flat hybrid field is the clean
    form: the S7 line's games enter the plain line's training and vice versa, as in every local field, and
    nothing else varies. Not the std field as context: ten lines, ~3x the cost, and the S7 model's games would
    enter eight other recipes' training. Cost ~1.5-2.5 CPU-h a run (ply-1 games for 4 entries, plus S7's
    training at 4-35x the plain update, a few seconds a cycle), ~4-5 CPU-h for both seeds.
(b) Reads against r + e + g + h @300 at v150, v300 and the final, plies 1 and 2; the verdict is S7 minus F-P
    against the FP-only face of the panel (same-kind contrast), the mixed and TR faces reported. Bands: ply 1
    30-120 if interactions matter on this data, under 15 closes the feature lever (and the architecture
    route at ply 1); ply 2, an off-ply read, 0-60. Two seeds resolve 50 cleanly and 30 marginally (6.97); a
    result of 15-40 at ply 1 gets a third seed before judgement. Diagnostics to log: pairs added per 1M and
    the running total, pairs dropped, the |z| of the selected candidates each round (a top |z| of 5-6 says a
    thin signal, 10-50 a strong one), the number of active pair features per position, and once, offline, the
    held-out full log-loss of S7 against plain on a P1 slice (0.01-0.03 nats expected if pairs matter; log-
    loss is the diagnostic, the arena the verdict, as every case so far has taught).
State: the candidate statistics (S, n; ~3 MB) are training state, not model parameters: a per-line STATE file
kept for the current version only, written every cycle as the trees' are, read for exact resume; the model
file holds only the pair list and the pair weights, so hybrid plays it and the library stores a model, not
hundreds of 3 MB statistic blocks. If the fork finds the state file costly, the alternative is to keep the
statistics in memory within a cycle and recompute nothing across versions, which breaks exact resume and is
not acceptable; the state file is the one way.

6.99 (02:00, 11-13). Agenda item 15: positional weakness (the user's own games: aimless openings such as 1. b3
2. a3 3. f3 4. h4; horizon blunders at ply 3 like 10...Qxa2 11. Nxc7+ Kd7 12. Nxa8; 10-1-1 against the models up
to ply 7-8).
What limits positional learning, ranked: (1) REPRESENTATION. Every model is additive over (square, piece)
indicators, partitioned by phase; positional concepts are interactions (pawn structure is pawn x pawn, shelter
is king x pawn, an open file is a rook x the emptiness of six squares), and an additive table cannot hold them
in any leaf; this is what the user saw. (2) THE LABEL. The game's result credits ~80 positions equally, and
between weak self-play opponents it is decided by short tactics, so the positional signal in the label is
buried under tactical noise; labels from the model's own search (--label-depth k, blended with the result by
--label-result) resolve the short tactics first and leave a label that is more about the position: TD-leaf-
like, zero knowledge (the model's own search). (3) COVERAGE and opponents: nothing in the data distinguishes
1. b3 from 1. e4 because the outcomes against these opponents do not depend on it; positional play needs
opponents who punish positional errors, which is self-play's circularity at this level; the ladder helps
(deeper play converts positional edges, 6.30). (4) The horizon blunders are search, not evaluation: a 3-ply
search with no quiescence; capture extensions were declined as knowledge, and a model-driven extension (extend
when the model's value swings by more than X between plies) is the allowed form.
Record on search labels: tested once, in the old regime (lr-settings, 11-09: F-L, mixed field with random
players, T 0.1): strongest in-run (confounded, it fed every other line), cold at T 0.02 48 +-46 over the best
rate line at ply 2 and no better at ply 3 (ply3-labels-cold, saturated against randoms); never in the current
regime, never with probit. It is a candidate.
Levers, ranked, with designs: 1. S7 pairs (approved, building): the only lever that makes positional concepts
representable; pawn-pawn and king-pawn pairs are structure and shelter; files need more than pairs and come
later if pairs pay. 2. Search labels, labels-p1: a two-line probit field at ply 1, F-P with result labels
against F-P with --label-depth 2 --label-result 0.5, two seeds as separate runs (flats are deterministic),
1,584 games, floor 300, cap 400; the label line's training is ~12x a flat's (a depth-2 search per position,
~30 s a cycle at ply 1), so ~3-4 CPU-h a seed. Read against the FP face of r + e + g + h at v150, v300, final,
plies 1 and 2. Band: labels minus result 20-80 at ply 1 and 30-100 at ply 2 (the label carries depth). Rule: 40
or more adopts search labels for the standard's flats and sends the test to ply 2; under 15 closes the label
lever. 3. Model-driven quiescence: a play-time lever read as an ARENA, no training: the same models at ply 3
with and against without an eval-swing extension, 4 models, minutes to an hour; band 50-150 at ply 3 on the
horizon-blunder account; "search last resort" makes it the user's call, and it is the cheapest fix for what
they saw. 4. The ladder (running): deeper training plies, the proven route.
A proxy for positional strength, offline and zero-knowledge: held-out log-loss on QUIET middlegame positions
of decided games, "quiet" defined by the model itself (its 1-ply and 2-ply search values agree within 0.05),
"middlegame" by move number (10-30), "balanced" by the flat's own value near 0.5 (no piece values enter); the
tactical positions are the ones a search resolves, so the quiet subset is where the evaluator's positional
judgement is tested alone. The occasional judge is a small deep arena (ply 6, four library models, ~100 games
each, 1-3 CPU-h), where search resolves tactics and the evaluator's positional quality decides more, which is
the user's own observation at plies 7-8 made measurable.
Now, given six runs and two forks: start nothing. labels-p1 queues behind residual-p2 and S7; the quiescence
arena is cheap and waits for the user's word on search.

6.100 (02:40, 11-13). S7 built (pairs1; byte-identical off; exact resume) and the selection statistic is broken
as the fork says: mean z^2 4.78 (design effect from within-game correlation), 12,005 candidates over |z| 5
against 0.2 expected, and 25 of the first 50 pairs sharing one value (a black pawn on h6): the component's
own unexplained marginal residual leaking into every pair that contains it, not an interaction. Offline: the
more pairs and the sooner, the worse (+0.0052 at 200k), the strongest 33 pairs -0.0002 +-0.0002.
1. Corrected statistic, both fixes, exact formulas. (b) INTERACTION CONTRAST: keep per-indicator-value
   accumulators (S_a, n_a for the 910 values; trivial) and the global (S, n); for a candidate pair ab the
   estimate is the additive model's excess, I_ab = S_ab / n_ab - S_a / n_a - S_b / n_b + S / n (the two-way
   interaction contrast; a component's marginal effect cancels by construction, so the h6 pawn can no longer
   carry its pairs). (a) CLUSTER-ROBUST SE: within a game the residuals v share the outcome and a static pair
   (two pawns) stays active for many consecutive positions, so its positions are one observation, not many;
   accumulate per candidate the game partial G_ab (the sum of v over the current game's positions where ab
   is active), and at the game's end S_ab += G_ab, Q_ab += G_ab^2, g_ab += 1; then SE_ab = sqrt(Q_ab) / n_ab
   and z_ab = I_ab / SE_ab (the pair term dominates the contrast's variance since n_ab << n_a, n_b). This
   needs the training stream GAME-CONTIGUOUS for the pair lines: shuffle games within a round, not positions
   (a training-order change for S7 lines only; plain lines keep their bytes). If contiguity is impossible,
   the fallback is a game count per candidate via a last-game-id stamp (g_ab) and z_ab = I_ab sqrt(g_ab) / s_v
   with s_v the residual SD, which treats a game's positions as one observation (conservative). Memory: S, Q,
   G, g per candidate, ~6 MB. Defaults: selection every 1M positions, k = 20 per selection (not 50), bar |z| 5
   on the corrected z (Bonferroni over 357k is 5.1), cap 500 pairs (the float path costs x2 at 300 and x3 at
   2,000, and the offline test says fewer is better), shrink 0.5, drop at |mu| under 0.5 sqrt(s2) after 3M own
   examples. Expected null behaviour, to check on a game-shuffled stationary pool before any launch: mean z^2
   1.0 +-0.1 over the candidates, about 0.2 candidates over |z| 5, and no indicator value represented among
   the selected pairs beyond its frequency; mean z^2 above 1.3 says the clustering is still leaking.
2. The 33-pair read says nothing about interactions yet: the broken statistic selected leakage and static-
   pawn pairs, and their failure to move held-out loss is the statistic's failure. After the fix, held-out
   loss IS a fair screen for this lever, unlike the path cells: pair features change the fit on the same
   positions rather than re-weighting calibration in decided ones; a genuine interaction gain should show as
   0.003-0.01 nats on the full held-out and more on the quiet middlegame subset of 6.99. Under 0.001 after the
   fix would say the lever is thin at ply 1 and predicts an arena result under 15; the arena still decides.
3. Wait for the fix and the offline rerun (minutes); do not launch the as-built drafts. With noise pairs
   admitted a null would be the statistic's, not the lever's, and the runs are cheap enough to do once right.
   The two-line draft at 768 games is fine. Launch when the null check passes and the offline rerun shows the
   corrected selection at least not worse than plain.

6.101 (03:10, 11-13). (1) Extensions: three runs met the plateau at their first rated cycle after the cap, as

the standard's data per cycle. The within-run contrast is still one factor; the bands stand at the plateau

with the standard's regime (volume ~20 at equal versions). S7's two-line drafts share the cap; same note.
Agenda item 9, thinking ahead: if residual leaves, S7 and search labels all land within noise, what remains,
ranked by expected gain per CPU-h under the constraints:
1. Model-driven search extensions (allowed: eval-swing extensions, PV ordering; "search last resort"): the
   user's own games show the weak part at plies 3+ is the SEARCH (horizon blunders with no quiescence), not
   the evaluator; a quiescence driven by the model's own value swing (extend a node when the value changes by
   more than X between plies) costs no training CPU and is read as an ARENA (the same library models at ply 3
   with and without it, minutes to an hour). Band 50-150 at ply 3. If it pays, the next step is to TRAIN with
   the extended search (a new training regime at ply 2-3), which is the ladder's lever and this one combined.
   Highest gain per CPU-h on the record; needs the user's word.
2. The ladder itself (the ply-3 and ply-4 rungs for the flats, 8-30 CPU-h a rung, +150-300 at the rung's
   ply): it does not beat the parameter-limited model, it IS that model trained deeper, but it serves the
   stated goal (an evaluator for deeper search) more than any architecture has, and it raises the bar every
   architecture must clear.
3. A factorized pair model (probit over rank-k embeddings of the indicators, every pair implicitly: the FM
   idea with ADF; the old FM was -307 in the old regime, LR-based and untuned): ONLY if S7 shows any signal
   (15 or more at ply 1, or 0.003 nats or more offline after the fix); then it is the scalable version of
   pairs. If S7 is null after the fix, features are closed on this data and the FM is not built.
4. The label's knobs (--label-result 0.25 against 0.5; --label-depth 3 at ~10x the label CPU): only if labels-
   p1 shows 20 or more; then the blend is a cheap one-factor at ply 1 (~3 CPU-h a seed).
5. On-policy volume for trees (the self-games share, one factor at ply 1, ~2 CPU-h): the 2 x 2 valued on-
   policy data at ~45, but the standard already gives each line its own games; expected 0-20. Low.
   Not worth it: ensembles of plateaued flats (seed replicates differ by 18; averaging buys under 10), probit
   alpha, longer runs at the plateau, more composition replicates.
   The honest line if all three in-flight levers fail: the parameter-limited flat trained at its ply is the
   model, the remaining strength is on the search side, and the user should hear that plainly rather than
   see a fourth architecture.
The offline proxy (held-out log-loss on quiet middlegame positions of decided games, 6.99): build it NOW, but
as a screen it is worth nothing until VALIDATED, since full log-loss failed three times (trees 0.05-0.09 nats
better and losing in play; cells and weighting the same). Validation: run it on 4-5 contrasts whose arena
answer is known (capped tree against flat at ply 1, +85; pure against mixed F-P at ply 2, +15; the ply-2-trained
tree against its flat at ply 2, -34; LR against probit flats, level; x4 against bfp, +15 within kind) on the
P1 and P2 held-out slices, minutes per model, no league CPU. If it gives the signs and rough order of all five
it is a screen and every new representation goes through it before a field; if it misorders any, log-loss
proxies are closed for good and the arena stays the only read. My expectation: it gets the within-kind cells
(flat against flat) right and the cross-kind cell (tree against flat at ply 2) wrong, because that cell is
matchup and lag, which no held-out set contains; a screen for REPRESENTATION changes within one kind, which
is what S7 and the FM are, is still useful at that standard.

6.107 (05:30, 11-13). residual-p2 at v150 (y + v + l @299): residual minus capped -57 at ply 2 and -50 at ply 1
(bands 20-60 and -20..20: below both); capped minus F-P +138 at ply 2, which is a WEAK F-P (-97 / -114 against
the panel mean, ~140 below the standard's ply-2 F-P at equal versions on the same scale) with the capped trees
only ~20 below the standard's; in-run (cold games) all six lines level.
(a) Mechanism for the residual deficit, pre-registered: two learners chasing each other. The shadow (trunk)
    trains on every example and keeps moving with the stream; the delta is a correction RELATIVE to the
    shadow's state, so every move of the shadow makes the delta stale, and with inherit-sums the delta's
    fast variances track those moves as noise; in a stationary pool the shadow converges and the delta
    settles, in a moving ply-2 field it never does. The design removed the leaf's lag behind the stream and
    added a lag behind the trunk. Prediction for v300 / final: the gap narrows as the field slows (residual
    minus capped -40 to -10 at ply 2) and never leads; a deficit still at -40 or worse at v300 closes residual
    leaves at ply 2 (the mechanism says they cannot win in a moving field), and a shrunk-delta variant (a slow
    delta, which would chase less and specialise less) is not worth building unless v300 shows the gap closing
    fast (to within 15). Nothing changes before v300.
(b) The weak F-P, twice (probit-field-p1 -90, residual-p2 -140 against the standard's F-P at ply 2), with its
    capped trees nearly level and in-run ratings level: this is not 6.102's narrowness as stated, because the
    narrowest field of all, fponly-p2 (four identical F-Ps), made the STRONGEST ply-2 flat against strangers.
    The common factor of the two weak cases is a field of F-P plus probit trees ONLY, and the signature (level
    in-run, -140 against strangers) is CO-ADAPTATION of 6.68's kind: the flat's whole experience is two
    styles, its deterministic twin and one tree recipe, and it fits a value function that beats those trees
    in-field and fails against strangers; the eight-recipe field dilutes that and the pure F-P field has no
    trees to fit to. 6.102's mechanism is amended to this; its v300 band for probit-field-p1 stands (-80..-30
    at ply 2 against the std), now with the prediction that the deficit is specific to the FLAT (the probit
    trees within 30). Test: no new run; one cheap arena: residual-p2's F-P@150 against its own field's trees
    direct (expected level, as in-run) and against the standard's and base-probit-p2's F-Ps and trees as
    strangers (expected -100 or worse); the direct-against-stranger gap on one model is the co-adaptation
    measured. For the standard this says the mixed eight-recipe field is right for flats and probit-only
    fields are a trap for them, which closes the ladder's "probit-only advantage" (6.96 D) on the flat side as
    well.

6.108 (05:55, 11-13). labels-p1 final (v400; both runs capped, not plateaued): search minus result labels at

(a) Ruling: the lever is RE-OPENED, for ply 2, despite the ply-1 letter. The pattern is what the label does
    by construction: a position's label becomes the value a depth-2 search by the model expects from it
    (blended half with the result), so the evaluator is trained to agree with what a 2-ply search finds, and
    that is exactly what a search at ply 2 asks of its leaves; at the root (ply 1) the result label is as good
    or better (the search label's own errors enter it). "Labels help the evaluator inside a search, not at
    the root" is the right summary, and ply 2 is the goal's dimension, so the ply-1 closure is a closure of
    the wrong cell. Both seeds agree at ply 2 (53 / 66), which one-run noise (~45) would not give twice.
(b) Next, cheapest first. 1. A ply-3 arena of the four finals (two result, two label F-Ps) with ply-3

    with the reading depth? Band: label minus result at ply 3 40-120 (the label is a depth-2 value; a 3-ply
    search sits one ply beyond it, same parity as ply 1, so I expect less than at ply 2 but positive); under
    15 says the gain is depth-2-specific (the label's depth sets the ply it helps), which is still useful: it
    says to label at the depth you will search. 2. A ply-2-TRAINED labels field: F-P result against F-P

    cycle under the cap; the label search at ply-2 positions costs ~12x the plain training, ~5-8 CPU-h a
    seed at ply 2), floor 300, cap 401, read against y + v + l @299 at plies 2 and 3. This is the one that
    matters for the goal: the ply-2 flat is the best evaluator we have at ply 2, and if search labels add 40
    or more to it there, the standard's flats take search labels at every rung. Band: label minus result 30-
    90 at ply 2, 20-80 at ply 3. --label-depth 3 is the follow-up, not the first arm (10x the label CPU; try

(a) Build pairs3 now: per-checkpoint restart of the statistics (fresh S, Q, G per selection window, so the
    contrast sees the component's marginal only as it stands in that window) and a frequency-aware SE (the

    the cap was reached by the wrong pairs); the drop rule stronger, |mu| under 1.0 sqrt(s2) after 3M own
    examples (one SD, not half: a pair that cannot be told from zero after 3M of its own examples is not
    carrying structure), so that inert pairs leave room for the next selection instead of holding the cap.
